# AHV package pages — FAQ proofread + objection-handling audit (23 Sep 2026)

Owner ask (23 Sep 2026): check all the package pages and their FAQs, proofread them, and brainstorm each so it handles the doubts of people who want that condition treated, especially international patients aged 40+. Answer to the question asked: **no — until this pass the FAQs had not been brainstormed per condition.** This pass does it for all 31 package pages. It is **findings only**: nothing on the site changed, because the fixes are clinical copy (HARD GATE 7, Dr. Deepu John's sign-off) and new drafts must come through content-geo-writer.

Method: one auditor per page read the whole server-rendered page (hero, sections, FAQ, FAQPage structured data), proofread it, and brainstormed as a 40–70-year-old international patient living with that condition. A second, sceptical agent re-checked every proofreading finding against the page and kept only real ones. Raw per-page output: `docs/reports/2026-09-23-ahv-package-faq-audit.json`.

## Totals

| Measure | Count |
|---|---|
| Package pages audited | 31 |
| Verified proofreading findings | 1324 |
| Objection-handling gaps (must / should / nice) | 738 (291 / 342 / 105) |
| Claims needing Dr. Deepu John's sign-off | 496 |
| Page verdicts (good / needs-work / weak) | 0 / 0 / 31 |

Finding kinds: locked-word 391 · overclaim-medical 210 · contradiction 181 · typo 154 · unclear 153 · grammar 150 · wrong-fact 78 · duplicate-question 7.

## Fix once, fixes many (shared template copy)

The package pages share template sections, so 68 findings repeat on 3+ pages. Fixing these in the shared views removes most of the total. 562 findings are page-specific.

|---|---|---|---|

It also worries a patient who wants the same physician throughout, and it appears on 30 pages. → "Your treating physician may change, subject to availability; your records and plan are shared across the medical team." (owner to confirm) |

## Per page

### /panchakarma-therapy/ — weak

Panchakarma: Ayurveda's classical five-procedure cleansing programme (Vamanam, Virechan, Vasti, Nasyam, Raktamokshana). It is a programme, not one disease. The page aims it at people with persistent digestive complaints, chronic joint problems or metabolic issues, and at anyone seeking "systemic renewal". The programme page sells it as a stay of at least 14 nights, priced per villa per night.

FAQ questions on the page: 5.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [overclaim-medical] “recognized for permanently resolving disease and restoring systemic balance” — This claims permanent resolution of disease, which is a cure claim in all but the banned word. It is not supportable and exposes the business on GST and clinical grounds. It is in the first paragraph a patient reads. **Fix:** Replace with physician-approved wording, e.g. "a classical Ayurvedic cleansing programme that aims to restore balance; your physician will explain what to expect for your condition". Needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [locked-word] “80% guest-trusted scientific detox for body, mind, and dosha balance | 30,000+ Lives Transformed” — Contains the banned word "guest". "80% guest-trusted" means nothing, and "scientific detox" and "30,000+ Lives Transformed" have no source. The same string is the og:title, so it appears on every social share. **Fix:** Use a factual line, e.g. "Physician-supervised Panchakarma at an NABH-accredited Ayurvedic hospital". Keep a number only if the owner supplies its source (HARD GATE 6). Fix the og:title too.
- [overclaim-medical] “87% of guests report dramatic health improvement within two weeks. 94% maintain benefits long-term” — Outcome percentages with no source, method or year. Also uses the banned word "guests". This line appears only on this page. **Fix:** Remove unless the owner provides an audited data source and Dr. Deepu John approves the wording. If kept, say "patients" and cite the source.
- [overclaim-medical] “Zero adverse reactions in 100,000+ therapies—safe, expert clinical care” — An absolute safety claim with no source. It contradicts /faqs-downloads/, which says some patients get mild detoxification symptoms such as headaches, fatigue or digestive changes. Induced emesis and purgation are strong bodily reactions by design. **Fix:** Remove. Replace with an honest monitoring statement, e.g. "Your physician monitors you daily and adjusts treatment if you feel unwell" (physician to approve).
- [unclear] “92% patient satisfaction among international clients (2024 data)” — A statistic with no named survey, sample size or source. The same figure appears on 3 programme pages. The /online-consultation/ page says the hospital does not publish curated quotes and sends readers to Google reviews, so the two pages take opposite positions. **Fix:** Cite the survey (owner) or remove it and link to the public Google reviews, as /online-consultation/ does.
- [overclaim-medical] “Drawing on 5,000 years of Ayurveda wisdom, Panchakarma is the gold standard for natural detoxification and rejuvenation.” — "Gold standard" is a superlative clinical claim. "5,000 years" contradicts /faqs-downloads/, which says Ayurveda's "texts date back over two thousand years". **Fix:** e.g. "Panchakarma is Ayurveda's classical five-part cleansing programme, described in texts over two thousand years old." The owner should pick one antiquity figure for the whole site.
- [contradiction] “Panchakarma might last anywhere from seven to twenty-one days.” — The FAQ's duration contradicts this page ("requiring a minimum two-week commitment"; "Villas — Minimum Duration is 14 Nights") and /faqs-downloads/ ("Panchakarma & Detoxification — from 14 nights"). A patient can't tell whether 7 nights is allowed. The same text is in the FAQPage JSON-LD. **Fix:** Physician or owner to set the rule, e.g. "Panchakarma at Kairali runs for a minimum of 14 nights; your physician may advise 21 nights for your condition." Change the accordion and the JSON-LD together.
- [contradiction] “Internal herbal medicines, lab tests, or specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed.” — On the Panchakarma package page itself, Panchakarma is listed as an extra-cost add-on. The patient cannot tell whether the nightly rate includes the procedures they are booking for. This is shared template copy found on 31 pages. **Fix:** Owner to state what this package includes, e.g. "Your Panchakarma procedures are included; internal medicines and lab tests are charged as prescribed." Remove the "(e.g., Panchakarma)" example on this page.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — Contradicts the same page: "Internal Medicines and test reports ... will cost extra", "Additional food items will be charged separately", and laundry, telephone and internet are charged extra. **Fix:** Drop "All-Inclusive" and "No hidden fees!". Instead list what the price covers and what is charged separately.
- [contradiction] “Internal Medicines and test reports needed for your treatment can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses and will cost extra.” — Contradicts /faqs-downloads/, which lists "Herbal medicines and formulations during your stay" as included in the package. No typical cost range is given either. **Fix:** Owner to decide whether medicines are included or extra, and align both pages. If extra, give an indicative range (needs owner).
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, which implies transfers are free. /faqs-downloads/ charges for them (Coimbatore Airport ₹6,775 MPV, one way). "Multilingual" does not say which languages. **Fix:** "Airport transfers can be arranged at extra cost (see How to Reach). Languages spoken: [owner to list]."
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — /faqs-downloads/ quotes different current rates ("valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027"), and the two windows overlap in October 2026. As of 23 Sep 2026 these tariffs expire in about 5 weeks, and no price is shown for a November admission. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the 2026–27 tariff and validity, then update this page and the price-list PDFs.
- [contradiction] “Led by 5th-generation Ayurvedic physicians, each treatment is reviewed for maximum safety and efficacy.” — Contradicts /faqs-downloads/, where the family "represents four generations". The physicians named on /online-consultation/ are BAMS physicians, not generations of the family. **Fix:** e.g. "Led by BAMS-qualified physicians under Chief Physician Dr. Deepu John". Owner to confirm the generation count.
- [overclaim-medical] “My inflammatory markers dropped 65% within 21 days, and I discontinued three medication courses.” — A lab result plus stopping medication: this reads as advice to come off medicines. The quote, attributed to "Sarah M., Germany", appears word for word on /personalized-customized-healing-program/ and sits next to a video of a bearded man. Its authenticity and consent cannot be verified. **Fix:** Remove unless the owner produces the patient's consent and source. Never publish claims that a patient stopped medication.
- [overclaim-medical] “They found issues missed by Western medicine. I am symptom-free after three weeks.” — A symptom-free outcome that disparages modern medicine. It is also reused word for word on /personalized-customized-healing-program/, and its authenticity is unverified. **Fix:** Remove or replace with a verified, consented patient quote that makes no outcome claim.
- [overclaim-medical] “It enhances immunity, digestion, and general health.” — FAQ 1: a flat medical benefit claim with no qualifier. It is also mirrored in the FAQPage JSON-LD, which feeds search results. **Fix:** e.g. "It aims to support digestion and overall wellbeing; your physician will explain what to expect for your condition." Fix the accordion and the JSON-LD.
- [wrong-fact] “our Ayurvedic specialists will evaluate your health and body composition.” — Wrong term: "body composition" means a fat and muscle measurement. The page means the Ayurvedic constitution (prakriti). **Fix:** "...will assess your health and body constitution (prakriti)." Fix in the JSON-LD too.
- [overclaim-medical] “Long-lasting advantages are ensured by adhering to the recommended post-treatment protocols following therapy.” — FAQ 5: "ensured" is a guarantee. "post-treatment ... following therapy" is redundant. The question also asks about precautions, and the answer names none (medication, pregnancy, etc.). **Fix:** "Following your physician's post-treatment diet and routine helps sustain the benefits." Add precautions written by the physician.
- [unclear] “panchakarma entails several therapies, such as herbal massages, steam baths, dietary changes, and cleansing procedures.” — FAQ 4 is vague and does not match the Process section (emesis, purgation, enemas). "Massages" is spa register. "panchakarma" is lower-case here but capitalised elsewhere. **Fix:** Physician to describe the three phases: preparation, the main procedure(s) the physician selects, and the recovery diet. Use "oil therapies (e.g. Abhyanga)" and capitalise Panchakarma.
- [overclaim-medical] “Nasyam: Herbal nasal therapies provide head, sinus, and migraine relief through targeted cleansing.” — Promises relief for a named condition (migraine) without qualification. **Fix:** e.g. "Nasyam: medicated nasal administration, used by our physicians for selected head and sinus complaints" (physician to approve).
- [unclear] “Raktamokshana: These therapies clean the body by removing toxins that cause skin problems and swelling.” — Never tells the patient that Raktamokshana is a blood-letting procedure, or whether KTAHV performs it. All five procedures are presented as steps everyone goes through. **Fix:** Physician to confirm whether it is offered and describe it plainly. Add "Your physician selects which of these procedures apply to you."
- [grammar] “At Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village includes the following steps:” — The sentence has no subject. **Fix:** "At Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village, Panchakarma can include the following procedures, selected by your physician:"
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Ungrammatical. It also worries a patient who wants the same physician throughout, and it appears on 30 pages. **Fix:** "Your treating physician may change, subject to availability; your records and plan are shared across the medical team." (owner to confirm)
- [unclear] “Book your personalized wellness retreat in India today.” — "Retreat" is hospitality register on a hospital page. It is not a banned word, but it undercuts the clinical lock. The same sentence is the meta description and the JSON-LD WebPage description. **Fix:** "Book a free consultation with a Kairali physician to plan your Panchakarma programme."
- [locked-word] “Trusted by guests worldwide, Kairali upholds this tradition with modern clinical excellence.” — Banned word "guests". **Fix:** "Trusted by patients from more than 120 countries..." (the 120 figure is from /online-consultation/) or just "patients".
- [locked-word] “Every protocol is custom-designed for the guest’s body constitution (prakriti), health needs, and goals.” — Banned word "guest". **Fix:** "...for the patient's body constitution (prakriti)..."
- [locked-word] “World Luxury Award Winner” — Banned word "luxury". It appears in the trust strip twice on this page and in the site-wide strip. **Fix:** Owner ruling needed. Suggest "NABH-accredited · Internationally recognised" or drop the item.
- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "...with continued physician support throughout your treatment."
- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Banned word "luxury". **Fix:** "Vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities"
- [locked-word] “Learn about the journey from seed to medicine.” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "See how medicinal herbs grow from seed to medicine."
- [locked-word] “Holistic Healing at Its Best: Hear our guest's Story from Kairali” — Banned word "guest", used in the video caption and the VideoObject JSON-LD name. Also "Story" is wrongly capitalised. **Fix:** On-page title: "Hear a patient's story from Kairali". Update the VideoObject name too.
- [locked-word] “A Journey to Health: Mr. Bikash Das’ transformational experience at Kairali” — Banned word "journey" in the H3, the video caption and the VideoObject JSON-LD. "Transformational" is also promotional. **Fix:** "Mr. Bikash Das on his Panchakarma at Kairali" (the on-page heading can differ from the YouTube title).
- [locked-word] “The serene surroundings and expert care made the journey incredibly soothing.” — Banned word "journey" inside a patient quote. **Fix:** Owner ruling: trim the quote with an ellipsis, or use a different verified quote. Do not silently rewrite a patient's words.
- [locked-word] “Premium luxury experience” — Banned word "luxury" (Royal Villa tagline). **Fix:** "Larger villa with additional space" (matches /faqs-downloads/).
- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "What your programme includes"
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility” — Banned word "luxury". **Fix:** "Accommodation: private, eco-friendly villas..."

There is also a typo: the award is "World", not "Worlds". **Fix:** Owner ruling on whether third-party award names with banned words may stay on KTAHV pages, e.g. move them to /awards-and-accreditations/ and keep NABH here. In any case fix "Worlds" to "World".
- [locked-word] “Acknowledged for its outstanding service and guest satisfaction” — Banned word "guest" (twice: Certificate of Excellence 2018 and 2019). **Fix:** "...outstanding service and patient satisfaction"
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024” — Typo: "Worlds" should be "World" (in the heading and image alt; the body text correctly says "World Luxury Hotel Awards"). The name also contains "Luxury". **Fix:** "World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024", subject to the same award-name ruling.
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The body says CONSIDICI but the heading says "COSIDICI National Award 2019". The image file is named ...COSIDICI-National-Awards-2016, so the year is uncertain. **Fix:** Spell it "COSIDICI" and have the owner confirm the year (2016 or 2019).
- [wrong-fact] “was honored as one of the 40 influential leaders in the wellness industry in 2018.” — The linked article slug says "most influential leaders under 40 by AsiaOne", so the text misstates the honour. **Fix:** "...was named among AsiaOne's most influential leaders under 40 (2018)". Owner to verify.

Likewise, the "Fit Premium certificate 2020" link points to the 2024 article. **Fix:** Correct both links or remove their "Read more" (owner to supply the right URLs).
- [typo] “South Asian Travel Awards 2018.” — Stray full stop in the heading and image alt. **Fix:** "South Asian Travel Awards 2018"
- [unclear] “socializing with fellow retreat participants.” — "Retreat participants" is hospitality register. **Fix:** "...or talking with fellow patients."
- [unclear] “(Note: Preventive Health & Regenerative Program includes one therapy per day.)” — Template text about a different programme, which confuses readers on the Panchakarma page. **Fix:** Remove on this page.
- [typo] “Join our Weekly fun, hands-on Ayurvedic cooking classes by in-house chefs.” — Stray capital W. "Fun" is a leisure-register word. **Fix:** "Join our weekly hands-on Ayurvedic cooking classes led by in-house chefs."
- [unclear] “Terms & Conditions — This package will also include” — The accordion heading calls the terms and conditions "inclusions". **Fix:** "Terms & Conditions"
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — "Up to less than" is ungrammatical. The line above it ("Up to 15 days before arrival date – 50% Refund.") is ambiguous about the 15–29-day window. **Fix:** Match /cancellation-and-refund-policy/: "30 or more days before arrival: full refund · 15–29 days: 50% refund · fewer than 15 days, or a date change during the stay: no refund."
- [unclear] “Extra Meals – Breakfast + Lunch + Dinner : INR 5,500.00” — No unit is given (per person? per day?). /faqs-downloads/ says per night. The format also differs from the ₹ figures elsewhere and has a stray space before the colon. **Fix:** "Extra meals (breakfast, lunch, dinner): ₹5,500 per person per night". Owner to confirm the unit.
- [unclear] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk has no footnote. The point is repeated in the T&C ("All rates and packages are inclusive of all taxes."). **Fix:** Remove the asterisk or add the footnote, and state the tax point once.
- [unclear] “For children below 5 years, stay and meals are Complimentary.” — Incomplete: /faqs-downloads/ adds that ages 5–12 pay 50% for food and extra bedding. "Complimentary" is wrongly capitalised. **Fix:** "Children under 5 stay and eat free; ages 5–12 are charged 50% for food and extra bedding; over-12s pay in full."

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 24 gaps):**
- Which of the five procedures will I actually have: all of them, including induced vomiting, purgation, medicated enemas and Raktamokshana (blood-letting), or only the ones my physician chooses? — _The Process section presents all five as "the following steps" of the programme. A 60-year-old reading "controlled emesis" and an unexplained "Raktamokshana" assumes the worst and does not book._ Source: needs physician/owner: Dr. Deepu John to state which procedures KTAHV performs, how they are selected, and whether blood-letting or leech therapy is offered
- I take blood-pressure, diabetes, thyroid, statin or blood-thinner medication. Can I keep taking it through the purgation and emesis days, and who adjusts my doses? — _Emesis and purgation cause fluid and electrolyte shifts and can change how pills are absorbed. The page's own testimonial suggests stopping medicines, which frightens careful patients and their GPs._ Source: The principle is on /online-consultation/ ("never stop a prescribed medicine without their guidance"). The Panchakarma-specific protocol needs physician/owner. The medication FAQ on /personalized-customized-healing-program/ needs physician verification before reuse.
- Am I too old or too unwell for Panchakarma? Who should not have it (heart disease, pacemaker, kidney disease, osteoporosis, recent surgery, cancer treatment, pregnancy)? — _FAQ 3 says it suits "the majority of people" but never names who is excluded. A patient over 60 with other conditions needs to know before paying for flights._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ (no stated maximum age; senior-patient considerations). The contraindication list itself needs physician/owner.
- I pay 100% in advance and fly in. If the physician then decides Panchakarma is not right for me, do I get a refund, a switch to a gentler programme, or nothing? — _The page requires full prepayment and gives no refund within 15 days of arrival. /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ gives no refund for early departure. This is the biggest financial fear for an older international patient._ Source: needs physician/owner (policy). Partial: /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ allows medical-emergency exceptions with management approval, and the free pre-screen on /online-consultation/ lowers the risk.
- How many nights do I need (7, 14 or 21), and what is the realistic total cost including the procedures, medicines and tests? — _The page gives 7–21 days in the FAQ and 14 nights minimum elsewhere, prices per villa per night, and even lists Panchakarma as an add-on. The patient cannot budget._ Source: This page (villa rates, 14-night minimum) and /faqs-downloads/ (Panchakarma from 14 nights). Whether the procedures are included, and an indicative cost for medicines and tests, needs physician/owner.
- What is a main-procedure day really like? How long do the vomiting or purgation last, am I confined to my villa, is a nurse or doctor with me, and is there a private toilet? — _The only daily routine shown is a generic wellness day (06:30 yoga, 11:00 nature walk), which cannot be true on emesis or purgation days. The unknown is what stops people booking._ Source: needs physician/owner. The attached bathroom per villa is stated on /faqs-downloads/.
- What side effects might I have (weakness, dehydration, dizziness, headache), and how are they managed? — _The page claims "Zero adverse reactions" while /faqs-downloads/ admits mild detoxification symptoms. The contradiction destroys trust. An honest answer builds it._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ ("Will I experience any side effects during treatment?"). Procedure-specific risks and how they are managed need physician/owner.
- If I become seriously unwell (chest pain, severe dehydration, low blood sugar), is a doctor on campus at night, and how far is the nearest emergency hospital? — _Patients aged 40–70 with other conditions are travelling to rural Palakkad. Emergency cover decides the booking for them and their families._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ (arrangements with nearby hospitals, 24-hour assistance, emergency services on call, advice to buy evacuation insurance). Night doctor or nurse cover and the nearest hospital's name and distance need physician/owner.
- Who will my physician be, what are their qualifications, will the same doctor see me throughout, and do they speak my language (German, French, Russian, Arabic)? — _The page says "Doctor might change as subject to availability" and mentions "multilingual staff" without naming a language. Continuity and language matter most on emesis and enema days._ Source: /online-consultation/ (Dr. Deepu John, Chief Physician & Head of Department, BAMS, 12+ years, helped draft the NABH Panchakarma Clinic Standards; Dr. Akhila Oommen and Dr. Rahul R, BAMS). Languages and continuity need physician/owner.

### /weight-loss-programme/ — weak

Overweight and obesity (Sthoulya) with metabolic risk. The programme is "Weight Management & Metabolism Therapy – Sthoulyaghna Chikitsa", minimum 14 nights. The page also names people with diabetes and cardiac risk factors as target patients.

FAQ questions on the page: 10.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the weight management programme.” — Banned word 'guests'. It also misdescribes the list: 6 of the 10 questions are about after discharge, not 'before starting'. **Fix:** Here are the questions patients most often ask about the weight management programme.
- [locked-word] “A personalized Ayurvedic wellness journey designed to gently improve metabolism, eliminate toxins, and promote sustainable weight balance—without crash diets or intense calorie counting.” — Banned word 'journey'. 'Eliminate toxins' is an unmeasured medical claim. The answer is a sentence fragment with no main verb. **Fix:** It is a physician-led Ayurvedic programme of at least 14 nights. It combines two daily therapies, a prescribed vegetarian diet, yoga and lifestyle guidance to support weight reduction, without crash diets or calorie counting. Any detox wording needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [contradiction] “The plan includes Panchakarma detox, herbal formulations, yoga, and nutrition aligned with classical Ayurveda.” — Contradicts this page's Inclusions ('Internal herbal medicines, lab tests, or specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed') and Process step 05 ('charged separately'). Panchakarma and herbal medicines are paid add-ons, not part of the plan. **Fix:** 'Your physician prescribes therapies such as Udwarthanam and herbal steam, plus diet and yoga. Herbal medicines and Panchakarma are added only if prescribed, and are charged separately.' Physician to confirm.

The diet is prescribed. Telling a diabetic patient 'No' is unsafe. **Fix:** 'Yes. Your physician prescribes a vegetarian diet for your programme, with no calorie counting. Smoking, alcohol and coffee are not permitted during treatment.' Physician to confirm the diet wording.
- [locked-word] “Most guests feel noticeable changes—better digestion, increased energy, and weight shifts—within 1–2 weeks of starting the program.” — Banned word 'guests'. 'Most' is an unsupported outcome claim and 'weight shifts' is vague. Any time frame or percentage needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [contradiction] “Our post-program support team is always available.” — 'Always available' reads as 24/7. /online-consultation/ promises 'a reply within one working day'. The answer also gives no contact details. **Fix:** 'Contact the patient care team on WhatsApp +91 92898 38797 or at info@kairali.com.' State a reply time only after the owner confirms it applies to post-discharge queries.
- [unclear] “Yes. - [contradiction] “We recommend a monthly virtual check-in for the first 3 months, then quarterly, to ensure your body stays balanced and results are long-lasting.” — Contradicts /faqs-downloads/, which says follow-ups are 'typically scheduled at 1, 3 and 6 months after your stay'. Half the FAQ is aftercare, and it never says whether follow-ups are free. **Fix:** Merge these into one question, 'What follow-up do I get after discharge (and is it charged)?', and use the freed slots for medication safety, eligibility, total cost and realistic results.
- [unclear] “Yes. The physician must decide whether Panchakarma is part of the weight protocol and whether it suits older or comorbid patients. No.' The diet is prescribed, and this matters for patients with diabetes on glucose-lowering drugs. · 'Who is This Program For?' lists 'Those with diabetes' and 'Desire for healthy cardiac function and lower risk factors' without any medication or contraindication guidance. The physician must define eligibility and exclusions. · Testimonials and headings: 'losing 70kg', 'overcoming depression' (a mental-health outcome), 'shed 4kg in just 7 days ... quick and healthy results', the 'OVER 100 INCHES FROM MY BODY' thumbnail, and 'Transformative Results'. These need patient consent, substantiation and an 'individual results vary' line. · 'Personalized Internal Medicines: Only physician-prescribed, in-house remedies (GMP-certified) for safe, targeted care'. This is a safety claim about the medicines. · 'Clinical Excellence: Led by 5th-generation Ayurvedic physicians, each treatment is reviewed for maximum safety and efficacy'. This is an efficacy claim, and the generation count conflicts with 'four generations' elsewhere. · FAQ follow-up protocol: 'monthly virtual check-in for the first 3 months, then quarterly, to ensure ... results are long-lasting' conflicts with /faqs-downloads/ '1, 3 and 6 months'. The physician must set one schedule and drop the guarantee. · FAQ: 'You can order prescribed Ayurvedic supplements and herbal formulations ... delivery to most locations worldwide'. This means continuing prescribed medicines abroad without a local doctor, and cross-border import legality is unconfirmed. · Process step 02 ('body-mind imbalances caused by stress or high work demands') is copied from a stress programme. The physician should write the actual weight-management assessment. · Missing and needs physician authorship before go-live: guidance on continuing allopathic medicines (especially insulin, sulfonylureas, GLP-1 agonists and antihypertensives), contraindications and BMI/comorbidity limits, and the realistic expected-results statement.

The FAQ also invites patients with chronic headaches, digestive problems and back pain. The page gives a 7-night villa minimum (programme listing: "7 nights", from INR 150,969). Two therapies a day (Abhyangam, Shirodhara, Udvarthana, herbal steam), with yoga, meditation and a sattvic diet. Panchakarma is listed only as a paid add-on.

The FAQ's "Medical support is on call 24/7" is vague and names no hospital or distance._ Source: FAQs & Downloads (arrangements with nearby hospitals; evacuation insurance recommended). ### /personalized-customized-healing-program/ — weak

Care for several chronic or lifestyle conditions at once: arthritis, diabetes, hypertension, digestive and hormonal disorders, multiple comorbidities and post-COVID recovery. This is a general inpatient programme (Swasthya Anukūla Chikitsa) that the physician tailors to each patient: 2 therapies a day, medicines, diet, yoga and meditation, 7-night minimum, prices per night by villa tier (valid to 31 Oct 2026). Note for the sitemap work: going by its content, this page is the umbrella entry for 'chronic / multiple conditions – not sure which programme'. The FAQPage JSON-LD (9 Q&As, matching the visible text) exists only inside the old view template. The server-rendered HTML (.next/server/app/personalized-customized-healing-program.html) and the prerender contain no FAQPage block, because schema.ts emits FAQPage only for /faqs-downloads/.

'Multilingual' names no languages, while the site says DE/FR are 'coming soon'. **Fix:** 'Airport transfers can be arranged at extra cost (see How to Reach). Staff languages: [owner to list].'
- [contradiction] “The program is designed for adults aged 18 to 65. Children under 12 are not eligible for therapies.” — Tells the core 66–70 audience the programme is not for them. FAQs & Downloads says 'There is no stated maximum age' and has a senior-patient section. It also says a child's eligibility 'depends on age and condition'. **Fix:** Dr. Deepu John to rule on age eligibility, e.g. no fixed upper limit with a pre-admission physician assessment over 65. Then align both pages.
- [contradiction] “Award-winning clinical establishment with 30,000+ lives transformed” — The same page says 'Over 100,000 patients from 50+ countries'. Elsewhere the site also says 50,000+. 'Lives transformed' is an outcome claim. **Fix:** Owner to confirm one patient count and use it site-wide, e.g. '30,000+ patients treated'.
- [contradiction] “Kairali represents more than 115 years of Ayurvedic healing, drawing from Kerala’s rich medical lineage.” — Stale figure. The same page shows '118 + Years Heritage' and 'since 1908', and the site uses 118+ about 176 times. **Fix:** 'more than 118 years (since 1908)'. Also fix '118 + Years' to '118+ Years'.
- [contradiction] “Led by 5th-generation Ayurvedic physicians, each treatment is reviewed for maximum safety and efficacy.” — FAQs & Downloads says 'four generations'. 'Maximum safety and efficacy' is a guarantee. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the generation count. Use: 'Each treatment plan is reviewed by the physician before and during your stay.'
- [contradiction] “Evening Meditation: Wind down with private guided mindfulness sessions in the evening.” — The next bullet says 'Private sessions are also available at an additional cost', and 'Daily Group Yoga' appears elsewhere. The heading is duplicated. **Fix:** Keep only the second bullet (group sessions included, private sessions extra).
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — The tariff expires about 5 weeks from today (23 Sep 2026), and there is no rate for 2027 stays. FAQs & Downloads cites 'current rates (valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027)' for the same add-on amounts. The programme data file says validTo 2026-09-30. **Fix:** Owner to publish the 2026–27 tariff with one validity window used everywhere.
- [unclear] “Double ₹42,470 per night” — It is not clear whether 'Double' means two patients both treated or one patient plus a companion. FAQs & Downloads says 'Rates are quoted per person per programme'. The 7-night total is not shown. **Fix:** Owner to confirm, then label it e.g. 'per night, two patients sharing, both on the programme' and show the 7-night total.
- [wrong-fact] “The World Health Organization reports traditional medicine in use across more than 170 of its 194 member states.” — WHO's figure is 170 of 194 Member States, not 'more than 170'. The same sentence continues with the banned word 'guests'. **Fix:** 'The WHO reports that 170 of its 194 Member States use traditional medicine. Here are the questions patients most often ask...' (verify against the WHO source).
- [locked-word] “Guests enjoy 2 Ayurvedic treatments daily, meticulously selected by our doctors” — Locked word 'guests'. The same word appears in 'the guest’s body constitution' and 'Guests valuing holistic therapies'. **Fix:** 'Patients receive two Ayurvedic therapies daily, prescribed by our physicians...'. Also 'the patient’s body constitution' and 'Patients who value...'.
- [locked-word] “Can Guests with Complex or Multiple Chronic Illnesses Join the Program?” — Locked word in the FAQ question and in its answer ('We welcome guests'). The legacy FAQPage JSON-LD carries the same text. **Fix:** 'Can Patients with Complex or Multiple Chronic Illnesses Join the Programme?' / 'We admit patients with...'
- [locked-word] “92% patient satisfaction from international guests (2024 data)” — Locked word 'guests', and the statistic has no source. **Fix:** 'international patients'. Owner to supply the survey source, or remove.
- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Locked word 'journey', and 'safe and effective' is a guarantee. **Fix:** '...with your physician monitoring you throughout treatment.'
- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Locked word 'journey'. It also appears in 'to tailor your journey' and 'Learn about the journey from seed to medicine'. **Fix:** 'Programme Inclusions' / 'to tailor your treatment' / 'how medicinal herbs go from seed to medicine'.
- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Locked word 'luxury'. It also appears in 'Luxury Stay:', 'Premium luxury experience' (Royal Villa) and 'World Luxury Award Winner' in the trust strip. **Fix:** 'Private, vastu-compliant villas', 'Your villa:', 'Our most spacious villa category'. Drop 'World Luxury Award Winner' from the trust strip.
- [locked-word] “crowned ‘Wellness Resort of the Year’ at the prestigious Asia Pacific Spa & Wellness Coalition (APSWC) Awards 2024.” — The awards block on a KTAHV clinical page uses the banned words spa, luxury and resort, and 'guest satisfaction': 'World Luxury Spa Awards', 'India’s Leading Spa Resort', 'leading wellness and spa brand'. **Fix:** Owner ruling needed on whether proper-noun award titles may stay on clinical pages. Recommendation: keep NABH and healthcare awards here and move tourism/spa awards to /awards/.
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — 'Worlds' should be 'World'. The same error is in 'Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024', and the body text says 'World'. **Fix:** 'World Luxury Spa Awards 2024' / 'World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024' (subject to the award-listing ruling above).
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The heading spells it COSIDICI, the body CONSIDICI. **Fix:** 'COSIDICI National Award'.
- [typo] “South Asian Travel Awards 2018.” — Stray full stop in the heading. Also 'Best Asian Healthcare Brands 2017' in the body vs the singular 'Brand' in the heading, 'View all recognitions ( 29 more)' with a space inside the bracket, and 'Organisations' vs 'Organisation' in the ATTO card. **Fix:** Remove the full stop and make the award names match between heading and body; '(29 more)'.
- [grammar] “At Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village includes the following steps:” — The sentence has no subject. **Fix:** 'At Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village, the programme includes the following steps:'
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Ungrammatical. It also raises a continuity-of-care worry without explaining it. **Fix:** 'Your treating physician may change, subject to availability; your notes transfer with you.' (owner to confirm)
- [grammar] “Bring: Cotton clothes, medications, recent reports & open mind” — Missing article. **Fix:** 'Bring: cotton clothes, your current medicines with a dose list, recent medical reports and an open mind.'
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — Garbled. The 'Up to 30 days / Up to 15 days' tiers are also ambiguous next to the Cancellation page's '30 days or more' / '15–30 days'. **Fix:** '30 or more days before admission: full refund; 15–30 days: 50%; fewer than 15 days, or a date change during your stay: no refund.'
- [typo] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk has no footnote. The tax statement is repeated three times, and 'Pricing :', 'Cancellation :' and 'Child Policy :' have a space before the colon. **Fix:** Remove the asterisk or add the footnote. State taxes once and remove the spaces before colons.
- [unclear] “to Customized Wellness” — The H2 reads as a fragment ('Our approach' is a separate eyebrow). Screen readers and the mobile audit record the H2 as 'to Customized Wellness'. **Fix:** One heading: 'Our Approach to Customised Care'.
- [contradiction] “Choose from 50+ therapies for multiple interconnected health conditions” — Contradicts 'selected by the physician': the patient does not choose. '50+' is unverified. **Fix:** 'Your physician selects from 50+ classical therapies...' (owner to confirm the count).
- [unclear] “NABH & AYUR Diamond Certified” — NABH accredits. FAQs & Downloads names Green Leaf (Kerala Tourism) and ISO instead. The issuer and name of 'AYUR Diamond' are unverified. **Fix:** 'NABH-accredited (Panchakarma)'. Owner to confirm the AYUR Diamond certificate before keeping it.
- [overclaim-medical] “Anyone preferring root-cause Ayurvedic treatments instead of symptom management” — 'Instead of' can read as replacing conventional diabetes or BP management. **Fix:** 'Patients who want Ayurvedic treatment alongside their existing medical care.'
- [overclaim-medical] “Tailored therapies and lifestyle plans for chronic issues, post-COVID recovery, and total rejuvenation.” — 'Total rejuvenation' is an absolute promise. The 'post-COVID era' framing is dated in 2026. The og:title adds 'India's Most Authentic Ayurvedic Treatment | 30,000+ Lives Transformed' (a superlative, with a double space and no diacritic). **Fix:** 'Physician-tailored Ayurvedic treatment for chronic and multiple health conditions.' Rewrite the og:title without the superlative.
- [unclear] “Ayurvedic lunch, focusing on a balanced meal with plenty of fresh vegetables, whole grains, and lean protein sources.” — 'Lean protein' reads as meat, but the kitchen is vegetarian. The routine also shows only one therapy slot (15:30) while two a day are promised, and says 'fellow retreat participants'. **Fix:** '...plant protein such as dal and legumes'. Show both therapy slots. Use 'fellow patients'.
- [unclear] “Check-in: 12:00 noon” — Hospitality register on a clinical page. 'Book Your Stay', 'massages', 'serene retreat' and 'Leisure Activities' have the same problem (not banned words, but off-register). **Fix:** 'Admission: 12:00 noon / Discharge: 10:00'. Use 'therapies' instead of 'massages'.
- [typo] “MORE PROGRAMs” — Wrong casing. The page also mixes 'Program' and 'programme' (the register requires programme), 'Udwartanam' and 'Udwarthana', and 'Bharathanatyam'. **Fix:** 'More Programmes'. Use 'programme' throughout, one transliteration of Udwartanam, and 'Bharatanatyam'.

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 25 gaps):**
- I am 68 and have diabetes and high blood pressure. Does the '18 to 65' limit exclude me, and what extra checks apply over 65? — _The core buyer is 40–70. The FAQ tells anyone over 65 the programme is not designed for them, while FAQs & Downloads says there is no maximum age. The likeliest buyer reads a 'no'._ Source: FAQs & Downloads (no stated maximum age; senior-patient considerations). The conflict needs a ruling from physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John).
- I take metformin or insulin, two BP tablets, a statin and a blood thinner. Who decides on any change? Will the herbal medicines interact? Will my sugar and BP be checked daily? — _The FAQ answer is generic, and a testimonial says someone 'discontinued three medication courses'. That is alarming for anyone on anticoagulants or insulin._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John): monitoring protocol and frequency, herb–drug interaction policy, and who may change allopathic doses.
- Who should not book this programme? For example after a recent heart attack or stroke, with uncontrolled diabetes, heart failure, kidney disease, active cancer, recent surgery or severe osteoporosis. — _'Who is this for' lists conditions but gives no exclusions. People with several conditions fear being sent home or harmed._ Source: needs physician/owner
- How many nights do I need for my combination of conditions? Is the 7-night minimum enough? — _The page sells a 7-night minimum but quotes results 'within 14 days'. The FAQ never states a recommended length for this programme._ Source: FAQs & Downloads (chronic-condition programmes 7–14 nights, most 14). The individual length is set by the physician at the free online consultation.
- What total should I budget? How much do internal medicines, lab tests and Panchakarma usually add? — _The page says 'All-Inclusive… No hidden fees' and then says medicines and tests 'will cost extra' and 'can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses'. All of this comes with 100% advance payment._ Source: needs physician/owner (typical cost range for add-ons)
- Does the 'Double' rate cover two patients who are both treated, or me plus a companion who is not treated? — _Couples in their 60s often travel together. The tariff shows 'per night' while FAQs & Downloads says rates are 'per person per programme'._ Source: needs physician/owner. Companion extra-bed and meal rates are on FAQs & Downloads.
- Is a doctor physically on campus at night, and how far is the nearest hospital with emergency and ICU care? — _Low blood sugar, BP spikes or chest pain are real risks in this age group. This page claims 'round-the-clock medical coverage', while FAQs & Downloads says 'Emergency medical services on call' and 'arrangements with nearby hospitals'._ Source: needs physician/owner (resident-doctor rota; name and distance of the partner hospital)
- What results can I realistically expect, by when, and what happens if it does not help? — _The 87%/94% figures are unverified. Leaving early earns no refund, so the patient is paying up front for an uncertain result._ Source: needs physician/owner for honest outcome wording and what is measured at admission and discharge. Cancellation & Refund Policy page for early departure (no refund for the unused portion).
- Who exactly are the physicians and what are their qualifications? Will one doctor follow me throughout? Do they speak German, French, Russian or Arabic? — _The page says 'Doctor might change as subject to availability' and 'multilingual staff' with no languages named. The DE/FR site is 'coming soon'._ Source: FAQs & Downloads (BAMS-qualified physicians; the Chief Physician oversees). Languages and physician continuity need owner.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** '87% symptom reduction within 14 days, 94% maintain improvements after 6 months': outcome statistics with no source. Needs audited data or removal. · 'Zero adverse reactions in 100,000+ therapies': absolute safety claim that contradicts the FAQs & Downloads side-effects answer. · '92% patient satisfaction from international guests (2024 data)': needs the survey source (owner). · Testimonial 'Sarah M., Germany': 'inflammatory markers dropped 65% within 21 days, and I discontinued three medication courses'. Implies stopping medication and cites a lab result. Authenticity and consent also need checking. · Testimonial 'Dr. Rajesh P., Mumbai': 'They found issues missed by Western medicine. I am symptom-free after three weeks.' Implies full resolution and disparages conventional care. · 'Why Choose' bullets: 'Relief from chronic/lifestyle disorders, improved immunity and digestion' and 'Hormonal balance, stress reduction, and enhanced mental clarity' are promised outcomes. · 'targeting underlying issues, not merely symptoms' and 'root-cause Ayurvedic treatments instead of symptom management': risk that patients replace conventional diabetes or BP care. · Hero and meta: 'chronic issues, post-COVID recovery, and total rejuvenation'; og:title 'India's Most Authentic Ayurvedic Treatment | 30,000+ Lives Transformed'. · 'Who is This Program For?' (arthritis, diabetes, hypertension, digestive, hormonal, post-COVID, multiple comorbidities): needs inclusion AND exclusion criteria approved by the physician. · FAQ age rule: 'adults aged 18 to 65… Children under 12 are not eligible… Adolescents aged 12 to 17 may participate in select therapies'. An eligibility rule that conflicts with FAQs & Downloads ('no stated maximum age'). · FAQ on multiple conditions: 'Treatment plans are carefully tailored… to ensure safety and compatibility with existing medical care'. · FAQ on medications: 'fully considered and integrated… We coordinate with your primary healthcare providers, only making adjustments with your consent and under strict supervision'. Who may alter allopathic drugs, and does coordination with home doctors actually happen? · FAQ on supervision: 'NABH-certified Ayurvedic doctors, ensuring safety and effectiveness throughout'. Wrong accreditation wording plus a guarantee. · FAQ 24/7: 'round-the-clock medical coverage by qualified professionals, ensuring immediate response'. Emergency capability must match reality (FAQs & Downloads says 'on call'). · 'Led by 5th-generation Ayurvedic physicians, each treatment is reviewed for maximum safety and efficacy'. · 'Modern diagnostic precision blends with ancient wisdom' and 'modern monitoring': which diagnostics actually exist on site? · 'Fresh, personalized medicines from on-site GMP-certified facility'. · Process step 04 therapy list (Pizhichil, Shirodhara, Navarakizhi, Udwartanam, Lepam, Nasyam, Vasti, Panchakarma) offered to a 40–70 patient group with several conditions: suitability and contraindications need stating. · 'Ongoing support ensures long-term health' and 'continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey'. · 'Unlimited personalized doctor consultations for complex comorbidities': a service promise the physician must confirm. · Routine detox claims: 'detoxifying drink to kickstart the metabolism', 'herbal-infused detox water' (minor).

### /digestive-wellness-gut-health-yoga-therapy/ — weak

Functional digestive complaints (IBS, acidity/hyperacidity/acid reflux/gastritis, bloating and gas, constipation, sluggish digestion or "Agni") managed with Ayurvedic treatment, yoga therapy (asanas, kriyas, pranayama) and diet. Residential, minimum stay 14 nights. Sanskrit name: Agni Dipanam Yoga Chikitsa.

FAQ questions on the page: 5.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [typo] “integrates carefully selected asanas, pranayama, and pranavmukthasna kriya to stimulate the digestive organs” — Misspelling. The same practice is spelled three ways on the page: 'pranavmukthasna kriya' (About), 'Pavanamukthasana Kriya' (Process 05, FAQ 2) and 'Pawanmukhtasana Kriya' (FAQ 1). **Fix:** Use one spelling everywhere, e.g. 'Pavanamuktasana Kriya', in the About text, Process step 05, FAQ 1, FAQ 2 and the FAQPage JSON-LD.
- [typo] “therapeutic yoga, such as Pawanmukhtasana Kriya and Kapalabhati” — FAQ 1 uses a third spelling of Pavanamuktasana. The JSON-LD copy has the same spelling. **Fix:** 'therapeutic yoga, such as Pavanamuktasana Kriya and Kapalabhati', in both the accordion and the JSON-LD.
- [wrong-fact] “a combination of Asanas like Pranayama, Pavanamukthasana Kriya, and Agnisara Kriya, along with Pranayama techniques such as Kapalabhati and Nadi Shodhana” — Factually wrong. Pranayama is breath work, not an asana, and Agnisara is a kriya, not an asana. 'Pranayama' also appears twice. Process step 05 repeats it too: 'Asanas, Pranayama, Pavanamukthasana Kriya, and Agnisara Kriya with Pranayama'. **Fix:** 'The programme combines asanas and kriyas such as Pavanamuktasana Kriya and Agnisara Kriya with pranayama such as Kapalabhati and Nadi Shodhana.' Fix Process step 05 the same way.
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the digestive wellness & gut health programme.” — Banned word 'guests' in the FAQ introduction. **Fix:** 'Here are the questions patients most often ask before starting the Digestive Wellness & Gut Health programme.'
- [wrong-fact] “The World Health Organization reports traditional medicine in use across more than 170 of its 194 member states.” — WHO's own wording (2019 Global Report on Traditional and Complementary Medicine) is 170 Member States, not 'more than 170'. The member-state count needs re-checking on the day of publishing. The statistic also says nothing about digestive care, so it adds nothing for a patient deciding on this programme. **Fix:** Either cite it exactly ('170 of 194 WHO Member States report use of traditional medicine', with the WHO report linked) or replace it with a programme-specific line such as 'Here are the questions patients ask before starting this programme.'
- [overclaim-medical] “For long-term problems, a 21-day program offers enhanced detoxification and gut healing with ongoing expert supervision and daily Yoga.” — Promises an outcome ('gut healing', 'enhanced detoxification'). The 21-day option is described nowhere else on the page, and nothing says whether it is priced at the same per-night rate. 'Yoga' has a stray capital. **Fix:** Rewrite after physician sign-off, e.g. 'For long-standing complaints, your physician may recommend 21 nights; this is decided at consultation.' The owner confirms how 21-night stays are priced.
- [overclaim-medical] “Effective for issues such as bloating, acidity, and IBS, it rejuvenates the digestive fire (Agni) and enhances overall vitality.” — Unqualified efficacy claim for named conditions (IBS, acidity). The same wording is in the FAQPage JSON-LD. **Fix:** 'It is designed for patients living with bloating, acidity or IBS; your physician confirms at consultation whether it suits you.' Pending Dr. Deepu John sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “this program provides substantial relief for issues like Irritable Bowel Syndrome (IBS), constipation, gas, acidity, and bloating, fostering long-term digestive wellness” — FAQ 3 opens with an unconditional 'Yes.' and promises 'substantial relief' and long-term results. It also goes further than 'Who is This Program For?', which limits suitability to 'IBS or mild digestive disorders', while FAQ 3 says 'chronic digestive issues'. **Fix:** Answer conditionally: which presentations the programme is suitable for, which need a gastroenterology review first, and what change patients typically notice, all worded by the physician. Pending Dr. Deepu John sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Heal IBS, acidity, bloating & more at Kairali’s gut wellness retreat in Kerala.” — The hero subtitle, meta description, og:description and JSON-LD WebPage description all promise to 'heal' IBS. 'Gut wellness retreat' is hospitality register on a hospital page. **Fix:** e.g. 'Physician-led Ayurveda, yoga therapy and diet for IBS, acidity and bloating at Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village, an Ayurvedic hospital in Palakkad, Kerala.' Update the meta, og and JSON-LD copies too. Pending sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Kairali’s natural treatment for digestive disorders guarantees enduring digestive health, grounded in nature” — Guaranteed outcome: a medical overclaim no physician can stand behind. **Fix:** Remove 'guarantees'. e.g. 'Kairali's programme aims to support lasting digestive health through treatment, diet and daily practice you continue at home.'
- [locked-word] “World Luxury Award Winner” — Banned word 'luxury' in our own trust-bar descriptor (shown twice: the hero trust row and the strip above the closing CTA). This is our copy, not an award title. **Fix:** Replace with a clinical trust signal, e.g. 'NABH-accredited' or '118+ Years of Ayurvedic Practice'.
- [locked-word] “crowned ‘Wellness Resort of the Year’ at the prestigious Asia Pacific Spa & Wellness Coalition (APSWC) Awards 2024” — The awards block has about 20 banned-word hits in award names and descriptions: 'Luxury Spa', 'Resort', 'India’s Leading Spa Resort', 'Luxury Yoga & Wellness Retreat', 'spa brand', 'Ayurveda Spa Continent Winner'. **Fix:** The owner decides whether proper-noun award titles are exempt. At minimum, rewrite our descriptive sentences in clinical register, or show awards as logos and years only on KTAHV clinical pages.

- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Banned word 'journey' in a section heading. It also appears in 'tailor your journey', 'healing journey', 'safe and effective journey' and 'the journey from seed to medicine'. **Fix:** 'What your programme includes'. Replace every other instance, e.g. 'tailor your treatment', 'your treatment', 'safe, effective treatment', 'from seed to medicine'.
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility” — Banned word 'luxury'. Also in 'Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities' and the Royal Villa tagline 'Premium luxury experience'. **Fix:** 'Accommodation: private, eco-friendly villas…', 'Vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities', and for Royal Villa 'Our most spacious villa' or similar factual wording.
- [locked-word] “Escape daily stress in our serene retreat.” — Holiday register ('escape', 'retreat') on a hospital page. 'escape' is on the extended list in language-lock-audit.json. 'retreat' also appears in the About text ('dedicated retreat'), the meta description and the footer. **Fix:** 'Step away from daily stressors while your physician and therapy team manage your treatment.'
- [locked-word] “About the Program” — The required register is 'programme'. The page mixes 'program' (About the Program, gut health program, Who is This Program For?, Pre-Program, FAQ 2/3/4/5) with 'programme' (FAQ intro, CTAs, trust bar). **Fix:** Use 'programme' throughout the visible copy and the JSON-LD.
- [contradiction] “Daily 2 Therapies Per Person” — Contradiction. Inclusions say 'Daily Rejuvenation: One Ayurvedic therapy per day, including herbal steam baths', and the daily routine shows one therapy slot (15:30). FAQs & Downloads says most programmes include two therapies a day. **Fix:** The owner or physician confirms the number of therapies per day for this programme, then the same figure goes in What We Offer, Inclusions and the Routine.

What We Offer says 'Daily Group Yoga Sessions' and the Routine says yoga asanas 'for 30-45 minutes'. - [contradiction] “Evening Meditation: Wind down with private guided mindfulness sessions in the evening.” — Listed twice. The second bullet says 'Private sessions are also available at an additional cost', so whether private meditation is included is contradicted. **Fix:** Keep one bullet: 'Evening meditation: guided group mindfulness or sound sessions; private sessions available at extra cost.' - [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — Contradicted on the same page. Also: 'rates are subject to change as per government policies' and a dangling asterisk in 'inclusive of applicable taxes*'. **Fix:** Replace 'All-Inclusive / No hidden fees' with a plain list of what is and is not in the per-night rate. Add an indicative medicines and tests range only if the owner supplies one. Resolve the asterisk.
- [contradiction] “Our therapies include abhyangam, dharas, podikizhi, navarakizhi, virechanam, matravasti,kashayavasti, etc, based on the body type and dosha balance.” — Virechanam and vasti are Panchakarma procedures, but Inclusions lists 'specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma)' as paid add-ons, so it is unclear whether they are included. - [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, which implies transfers are free. FAQs & Downloads says 'airport/railway transfers' are an additional service at extra cost. It also does not say which languages the staff speak. **Fix:** The owner confirms whether transfers are included or paid (and the price), and lists the languages actually spoken by physicians and patient care staff.
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — Contradicts the programme listing data (long-form-programme-set.json: this programme 'validTo 2026-09-30'). As of 23 Sep 2026 either date is close, and neither says what rates apply after it. **Fix:** The owner confirms the real validity date and publishes next-season rates, or states 'rates for arrivals after <date> on request'.
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — 'Up to less than' is garbled. 'Up to 30 days before arrival – Full Refund' reads as 'within 30 days', the opposite of what is probably meant, so the refund bands are ambiguous. **Fix:** '30 or more days before arrival: full refund. 15–29 days before arrival: 50% refund. Fewer than 15 days before arrival, or a date change during the stay: no refund.' Check it against /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ first.
- [contradiction] “NABH-accredited for Panchakarma — certificate AH-2024-0250, valid 25 April 2024 through 24 April 2027.” — The trust bar says '◆NABH Certified Hospital', but the certificate note says the accreditation is 'for Panchakarma'. - [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — 'Worlds' is wrong. The organisers' names are 'World Luxury Spa Awards' and 'World Luxury Hotel Awards'. The same error is in 'Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024'. (Banned-word ruling is separate.) **Fix:** 'World Luxury Spa Awards 2024' and 'World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024', subject to the owner's banned-word ruling.
- [duplicate-question] “Can this program help with chronic digestive issues like IBS, bloating, or acidity?” — FAQ 1 already answers this ('Effective for issues such as bloating, acidity, and IBS'), so FAQ 1 and FAQ 3 overlap and only 5 FAQ slots are used. Meanwhile safety, medication, reports, follow-up and cost questions are missing. **Fix:** Keep FAQ 1 as a definition only, and rework FAQ 3 into 'Is this programme suitable for my condition?' with physician-approved inclusion and exclusion criteria.

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 22 gaps):**
- I take a proton-pump inhibitor (omeprazole/pantoprazole), antispasmodics or laxatives, plus blood-pressure, diabetes, statin or blood-thinner medicines. Do I keep taking them, and will your herbal medicines interact with them? — _Most 40–70-year-olds with reflux or IBS are on long-term PPIs and other prescriptions. Fear of being told to stop them, or of herb–drug interactions, is the top reason they won't book, and the page never mentions it._ Source: needs physician/owner. A model answer already exists on another site page: /personalized-customized-healing-program/ FAQ 'How Does the Program Accommodate My Existing Medications?'. It must be re-signed by Dr. Deepu John for this condition. This page only says 'Bring: … medications'.
- I also have high blood pressure, heart disease, a hernia or hiatus hernia, a peptic ulcer, or recent abdominal surgery. Is it safe for me to do Kapalabhati, Agnisara Kriya, Pavanamuktasana, and therapies like virechanam (purgation) or vasti (enema)? — _The page offers vigorous abdominal kriyas and Panchakarma purgation/enema procedures to an audience that often has these conditions, and says nothing about screening or contraindications. A cautious patient reads that as either risky or not medically serious._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John: contraindication and screening list for this programme's yoga and Panchakarma elements)
- Which digestive conditions are NOT suitable (Crohn's, ulcerative colitis, coeliac disease, bleeding, unexplained weight loss, new bowel changes after 50)? Should I have a colonoscopy or gastroenterologist review before I come? — _Patients over 50 in Europe and the USA are routinely told new bowel symptoms need a colonoscopy first. A page that promises to 'heal IBS' without exclusion criteria loses the medically literate patient, and could admit someone who needs other care._ Source: needs physician/owner (inclusion/exclusion and red-flag criteria)
- Which reports should I send before booking (endoscopy, colonoscopy, stool tests, H. pylori, blood work)? Will the physician review my European/US reports in the free consultation? — _International patients arrive with a folder of tests and want to know they will be read and used, not replaced by pulse diagnosis alone._ Source: This page partly answers it ('Pre-Program: Teleconsultation with an Ayurvedic doctor', 'Bring: … recent reports'). The specific report list needs physician/owner. The booking route is the /online-consultation/ page.
- Who exactly will be my physician (name, BAMS/MD Ayurveda, experience with digestive disorders), and do they speak English, German, French, Russian or Arabic? — _The page says only 'Doctor might change as subject to availability' and 'multilingual staff'. A patient paying for 14 nights abroad wants to know who is responsible for them and whether they can describe their symptoms accurately._ Source: FAQs & Downloads (/faqs-downloads/) states treatment is led by BAMS-qualified physicians. Named physicians and the languages they speak need physician/owner.
- What will my day actually look like on this programme: how many therapies a day (one or two), is yoga private 2 hours or group 30–45 minutes, and are virechanam/vasti included? — _The page contradicts itself on all three points (What We Offer vs Inclusions vs Process vs Routine), and the Routine shown is a generic template. Uncertainty about what they are paying for stops bookings._ Source: needs physician/owner to reconcile. The page's Process, What We Offer, Inclusions and Routine sections then need aligning.
- What will the total cost be? Roughly how much do internal medicines, lab tests and any Panchakarma procedures add on top of the per-night rate? — _The page says 'All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees!' and also that medicines, tests and specialty therapies cost extra 'as your treatment progresses'. The 14-night minimum (from ₹318,920 single, Deluxe) is already a large sum, and an open-ended add-on bill is a major objection._ Source: This page (per-night tariffs, 14-night minimum, add-on list). An indicative medicines and tests range needs owner. FAQs & Downloads covers payment methods.
- How soon will I notice a difference, what counts as improvement, and how will I know whether 14 nights is enough or I will be told to stay 21? — _The FAQ gives only 'at least 14 days for significant improvement' and '21-day … gut healing'. Patients want realistic expectations and fear being upsold on arrival._ Source: needs physician/owner (realistic expectations and how extension decisions are made). FAQs & Downloads gives general stay lengths by programme type.
- What happens after I fly home: how many follow-up consultations, online or in person, are they included, and can I get my herbal medicines shipped to Europe, the USA, Russia or the Gulf? — _Gut complaints come back without continued diet and medicine. The page promises a 'Continuous post-discharge wellness plan' and 'Ongoing support' but gives no number of follow-ups, format, cost or how to obtain medicines abroad (customs rules vary)._ Source: needs physician/owner (follow-up schedule and cost, medicine supply and export). The /online-consultation/ page covers the teleconsultation format.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** Hero subtitle, meta description, og:description and JSON-LD WebPage description: 'Heal IBS, acidity, bloating & more': a promise to heal named conditions. · Approach section: 'guarantees enduring digestive health': a guaranteed outcome. · FAQ 1 (accordion and FAQPage JSON-LD): 'Effective for issues such as bloating, acidity, and IBS': an unqualified efficacy claim. · FAQ 3: unconditional 'Yes' and 'provides substantial relief for … IBS, constipation, gas, acidity, and bloating, fostering long-term digestive wellness'. It also widens suitability from 'mild digestive disorders' to 'chronic digestive issues'. · FAQ 5: 'Our doctors suggest at least 14 days for significant improvement' and '21-day program offers enhanced detoxification and gut healing': outcome and timeframe claims attributed to the physicians. · FAQ 2: yoga practices 'support liver function': a physiological claim. · Benefits list: 'Detoxifies the digestive tract naturally', 'Promotes gut flora balance and immune health', 'Enhances nutrient absorption and metabolism', 'Supports weight management', 'Encourages emotional balance linked to gut-brain connection', 'Restores overall gut harmony for long-term wellness'. · About and Approach: 'remove toxins', 'encourages natural recovery, enhanced metabolism', 'Your gut is the key to well-being'. · Process 02: Nadi and Darshana Pariksha 'identify the root cause of your condition'. It does not mention reviewing prior investigations (endoscopy, colonoscopy, stool or H. pylori tests). · Process 04: virechanam (therapeutic purgation), matravasti and kashayavasti (medicated enemas) listed for a 40–70 audience with IBS/constipation. Suitability, dehydration risk, interaction with diuretics or antihypertensives, and whether these are routinely part of this programme need physician sign-off. · Yoga content (FAQ 1, FAQ 2, Process 05): Kapalabhati, Agnisara Kriya and Pavanamuktasana are offered while 'Who is This Program For?' explicitly invites patients with acid reflux, hyperacidity and gastritis. Yoga-therapy texts commonly caution these kriyas in peptic ulcer, hernia, hypertension, heart disease and after recent abdominal surgery. The physician must confirm the screening rule and a contraindication line. · 'Who is This Program For?' lists IBS, acid reflux, gastritis and 'lifestyle-related digestive imbalances' with no exclusion or red-flag criteria (IBD, coeliac disease, GI bleeding, unexplained weight loss, new bowel change over 50) and no advice to get a conventional work-up first. · 'We follow Ayurvedic principles using natural therapies and herbs to minimize side effects': implies natural means fewer side effects. · Doctor Guidance: 'continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey' and 'Ongoing support ensures long-term health': safety and outcome assurances. · Testimonials under 'Transformative Results' are Panchakarma patients ('improved digestion, better sleep') shown on this programme's page. Outcome testimonials on a clinical page need physician/compliance sign-off and must be from this programme. · Routine diet guidance ('cooked oats with fruits', 'fresh juice', 'lean protein sources', 'warm herbal bath') is a generic template, not reviewed for gut patients (e.g. reflux, IBS, FODMAP sensitivity). The physician should confirm or replace it with programme-specific guidance. · No statement anywhere on the page about continuing or adjusting existing conventional medication (PPIs, antispasmodics, laxatives, anticoagulants, antidiabetics). Any answer added must be physician-authored.

### /yoga-based-stress-energy-reset-program/ — weak

Chronic stress, burnout and anxiety, low energy, fatigue and poor sleep. The page also lists tension-related high blood pressure, post-illness recovery and mood swings. Read by its content, not its name, this is a physician-led residential treatment programme where yoga, pranayama and meditation lead. It has consultation plus Nadi/Darshana assessment, 2 Ayurvedic therapies a day (Shirodhara, Thalapotichil, Nasyam, Kizhi), herbal medicines and a daily consultation. It is not a yoga course. For the sitemap: site-tree.json already files it under Treatments & programmes > Stress, sleep & headaches, which matches the content. It sits next to /treatment-to-remove-stress-and-strain/, which has the same 7-night minimum and the same villa tariff, and /sleep-restoration-therapy/. Yoga & Training should only link to it, not be where it lives.

### /immunity-enhancement-executive-health-assessment-therapy/ — weak

Ojasvardhana Chikitsa: a 7-night-minimum preventive programme for executives and busy professionals with low immunity, chronic fatigue, stress or burnout, or recovering from illness or viral infection. It combines an "executive health assessment" with Ayurvedic therapies, diet, yoga and meditation. Per the page and RATES_RECKONER Tier 3, the minimum stay is 7 nights, charged per night by villa type.
