- [locked-word] “Kairali is more than a wellness retreat” — The FAQ Q5 question and answer both use "retreat". A hospital should say what it is, not what it is "more than". **Fix:** Question: "How is Kairali's programme different from a general wellness stay?" Answer opens: "Kairali is an NABH-accredited Ayurvedic hospital…" Apply the same edit in the FAQPage JSON-LD.
- [overclaim-medical] “a tranquil, toxin-free setting” — "Toxin-free" is an absolute claim that can't be verified. **Fix:** Drop "toxin-free": "…in a quiet setting amid Kerala's greenery."
- [contradiction] “It provides a comprehensive health check-up and personalised wellness interventions.” — The hero, meta description and About section promise a "comprehensive health check-up" and "modern diagnostic insight". FAQ Q4 defines the assessment as Ayurvedic only (Nadi Pariksha, dosha, lifestyle, diet). The inclusions and T&C say lab tests cost extra and are only "ascertained as your treatment progresses". The page's core promise is never defined. **Fix:** State exactly what the assessment contains (which tests, if any, and whether they're in the rate). If lab work is extra, say so up front. Needs physician/owner.
- [overclaim-medical] “combines traditional healing sciences with modern diagnostic insight” — No modern diagnostic is named anywhere on the page, so the claim has nothing behind it. **Fix:** Name the diagnostics, or drop the claim. Needs physician.
- [contradiction] “Our treatments emphasize detoxification (Panchakarma)” — FAQ Q2 says the programme centres on Panchakarma. The inclusions list Panchakarma as a paid add-on ("specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed"). Sitewide FAQ q-13/q-14 put Panchakarma at 14 nights minimum, but this programme's minimum is 7. **Fix:** Physician to state whether any Panchakarma procedures are part of the 7-night protocol. Align FAQ Q2, the inclusions and the JSON-LD.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees!” — The same page charges extra for internal medicines, lab tests, specialty therapies, laundry, telephone and internet. Airport transfers are separately priced in the reckoner. **Fix:** "Includes accommodation, meals, prescribed daily therapies and applicable taxes. Internal medicines, lab tests, specialty therapies, laundry and transfers are charged separately."
- [wrong-fact] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, but the reckoner prices transfers separately (e.g. Coimbatore Airport MPV ₹6,775 one way). "Multilingual staff" is unverified: the sitewide FAQ marks staff languages as no-recorded-fact. **Fix:** "Airport transfers available at extra cost (see How to Reach)." Remove "multilingual staff" until the owner confirms which languages.
- [wrong-fact] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — The reckoner's 25–26 T&C says "through September 30, 2026". These rates also expire 7 days from today (23 Sep 2026), and the reckoner has no 2026–27 treatment rate list. **Fix:** Change the end date to 30 Sep 2026 now. Owner to supply the 2026–27 treatment rates before 1 Oct.
- [wrong-fact] “Reservations will be confirmed against 100% advance payments.” — Contradicts the reckoner's 25–26 T&C: a 40% deposit secures the booking and the balance is due 30 days before admission. Full payment applies only to specific-room requests made 6 months ahead. **Fix:** Owner to confirm, then align with the reckoner wording.
- [unclear] “Up to 15 days before arrival date – 50% Refund.” — Unclear and overlaps the 30-day tier. The reckoner says 15–30 days: 50%. **Fix:** "30 or more days before admission: full refund · 15–29 days: 50% refund · under 15 days: no refund." Owner to fix the day-30 boundary.
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — "Up to less than" is ungrammatical. **Fix:** "Less than 15 days before admission, or a date change during your stay: no refund."
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Ungrammatical, and uses the off-register word "doctor". **Fix:** "Your physician may change, subject to availability."
- [unclear] “₹21,567” — The INR per-night rates (₹21,567 through ₹65,033) have no source row in RATES_RECKONER.md, whose 25–26 treatment lists are USD/EUR only. The USD figures ($308/$572…) do match Tier 3. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the INR source. Never round or derive (reckoner rule 4).
- [typo] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk has no footnote. **Fix:** Remove the asterisk or add the footnote.
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — "Worlds" is a typo. The same "Best Luxury Yoga & Wellness Retreat (Global Win)" 2024 is credited to two different bodies ("Worlds Luxury Spa Awards" and "Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards"). The reckoner records it once, as World Luxury Awards 2024. **Fix:** Owner to verify which body gave it, then merge into one correctly named card.
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The body text spells it CONSIDICI; the card title says "COSIDICI". **Fix:** "COSIDICI" in both places, after the owner verifies.
- [unclear] “NABH Certified Hospital” — NABH accredits; it doesn't certify. The certificate on this page is scoped "for Panchakarma" (AH-2024-0250), so a hospital-wide "certified" claim is imprecise. **Fix:** "NABH-accredited (Panchakarma)", or wording the owner rules on.
- [overclaim-medical] “Transformative Results” — The heading promises outcomes, and both testimonials below it are about Panchakarma, not this programme. **Fix:** "What patients say", with testimonials from this programme's patients (owner to supply).
- [grammar] “Our skilled Doctors perform pulse examination (Nadi Pariksha)” — "Doctors" has a stray capital, and the FAQ pass (F38) standardises on "physicians". **Fix:** "Our physicians perform pulse examination (Nadi Pariksha)…", with the same edit in the JSON-LD.
- [typo] “MORE PROGRAMs” — Broken casing. The page mixes "program" and "programme" ("About the Program", "Who is This Program For?", "Pre-Program"); the FAQ pass (F35) standardises on "programme". **Fix:** "More programmes", and use "programme" throughout.
- [unclear] “History” — The section-nav item "History" jumps to "Our approach to…". The page has no history section. **Fix:** Rename the nav item "Approach".
- [locked-word] “A serene retreat in India offering traditional Ayurvedic treatments” — Sitewide footer copy in the hospitality register ("retreat", "relaxation"). **Fix:** Fix once in the shared chrome: "An NABH-accredited Ayurvedic hospital in Palakkad, Kerala…"

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 26 gaps):**
- Exactly which tests are in the "Executive Health Assessment" (blood panel, ECG, imaging, a written report), and are they in the per-night rate? — _A 50-year-old executive reads "comprehensive health check-up" as a corporate medical screening like the one at home. The page's own FAQ defines the assessment as pulse, dosha and lifestyle only, and the T&C say lab tests cost extra. That gap turns into a complaint or refund dispute._ Source: needs physician/owner: the test list, in-house or partner lab and its accreditation, and the price. This page only says "lab tests… available as needed" at extra cost.
- I take prescription drugs (blood pressure, statins, diabetes, thyroid, blood thinners, immunosuppressants). Can I keep taking them alongside the herbs you name (Ashwagandha, Guduchi, Amalaki)? — _Most 40–70-year-old patients are on medication. The FAQ names specific herbs, and that sends patients to research interactions._ Source: needs physician. The sitewide FAQ addition "Can I continue my current (allopathic) medicines…" exists but is marked no-recorded-fact, sign-off Dr. Deepu John.
- I have an autoimmune condition, am recovering from a serious viral illness, or have a cancer history. Is an "immunity-boosting" programme safe for me, and who should not book it? — _"Boosting immunity" alarms anyone with autoimmune disease or on immunosuppressants. The page targets "people recovering from illness or viral infections" but lists no contraindications._ Source: needs physician. For cancer, the separate /revitalizing-and-strengthening-treatment-for-cancer-patients/ programme exists. The sitewide "Which conditions do you NOT treat?" is no-recorded-fact.
- How many nights is the programme, and what will the whole stay cost? — _The only duration is "Villas — Minimum Duration is 7 Nights", buried in the tariff block. There is no total, and per-night pricing is easy to misread as room-only._ Source: this page (7-night minimum, per-night rates) plus RATES_RECKONER Tier 3. The recommended length beyond the minimum needs a physician; showing a bundled total needs an Abilash ruling (reckoner pricing rule).
- Is Panchakarma part of this programme, or an extra charge? — _FAQ Q2 says the programme emphasises Panchakarma, the inclusions call it a paid add-on, and Panchakarma's own minimum is 14 nights against this programme's 7. A patient can't tell what they're buying._ Source: needs physician/owner
- What results can I realistically expect, how soon, and how will you measure them? — _An executive taking 1–2 weeks off wants something measurable. The page lists ten benefits with no timeline or measure, and outcome promises would be an overclaim._ Source: needs physician
- Who will my physician be, what are their qualifications, and will they speak my language (German, French, Russian, Arabic)? — _The page names no physician, says "Doctor might change", and claims "multilingual staff" without naming languages. The DE/FR pages are "coming soon"._ Source: docs/group-inputs/kairali-physician-roster.md (5 BAMS physicians on the homepage's "Our Team of Ayurvedic Doctors"), /online-consultation/, and /faqs-downloads/ q-06 and q-11. Languages: needs owner (no-recorded-fact).
- What happens in a medical emergency? Is a doctor on site at night, and where is the nearest modern hospital? — _A 60–70-year-old with comorbidities is flying more than 10 hours to rural Palakkad. The page says nothing about emergency cover._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ q-37 (arrangements with nearby hospitals; evacuation insurance advised) and q-35 (emergency services on call). Night/weekend physician cover and the hospital's name and distance: needs owner (no-recorded-fact).
- How much do I pay up front, and what do I get back if I cancel, my visa is refused, or I fall ill before travel? — _The page demands 100% in advance, but the source T&C say a 40% deposit. International travellers carry visa and health risk._ Source: RATES_RECKONER 25–26 T&C (40% deposit, balance 30 days before; refunds at 30+/15–30/<15 days) and /cancellation-and-refund-policy/. Visa refusal: needs owner (no-recorded-fact).

**For Dr. Deepu John:** Benefit list, stated as outcomes: "Boosts immune response (Ojas) and overall vitality", "Detoxifies the body (Ama) and improves metabolic functions", "Reduces stress, anxiety, and mental fatigue", "Supports better sleep and emotional well-being", "Improves digestion and gut health (Agni)". Equating Ojas with "immune response" also needs physician wording. · Diagnostic claims: "comprehensive health check-up", "modern diagnostic insight", "comprehensive diagnostics", "extensive health evaluation", "Helps identify early signs of health imbalances". The physician must define what the assessment detects and which tests (if any) are included. · "Through pulse examination (Nadi Pariksha) and visual examination (Darshana Pariksha), we assess the dosha imbalance and identify the root cause of your condition": a root-cause diagnostic claim. · Indications in "Who is This Program For?": "Individuals with low immunity or chronic fatigue" and "People recovering from illness or viral infections". These need contraindications (autoimmune disease, immunosuppressant use, active infection, cancer) before they ship. · FAQ Q2: "immunity-boosting herbs such as Ashwagandha, Amalaki, and Guduchi". Naming specific herbs as immunity-boosting needs sign-off, plus a statement on use alongside prescription drugs. · FAQ Q2: "Our treatments emphasize detoxification (Panchakarma)". The physician must confirm whether Panchakarma is part of the 7-night protocol; the inclusions call it a paid add-on. · Process step 04: therapies listed as "corrective" (abhyangam, podikizhi, navarakizhi, shirodhara, ksheeradhara, nasyam). Confirm they are this programme's protocol and how many per day (the page says both 2/day and 1/day). · FAQ Q1 and Q3: "fortify the immune system", "revitalize the body", "profound rejuvenation and insight". FAQ Q2: "promote enduring vitality and strength". · "We follow Ayurvedic principles using natural therapies and herbs to minimize side effects": an implied safety claim. · "a tranquil, toxin-free setting" and "Our tranquil, restorative surroundings in Kerala enhance the healing effects, providing total rejuvenation for both the body and mind". · Post-treatment: "Ongoing support ensures long-term health" and "for sustained benefits". This is a durability promise. · "Rejuvenates the body with Rasayana (rejuvenation) therapies": confirm Rasayana is part of this programme's protocol. · The "Transformative Results" heading, with Panchakarma testimonials claiming "improved digestion, better sleep, and renewed energy", presented on a different programme's page. · FAQ intro statistic: "traditional medicine in use across more than 170 of its 194 member states". WHO's published figure is 170, so it needs a source or removal. · The five FAQ answers are duplicated verbatim in the FAQPage JSON-LD (view helmet in public/ahv/views/ahv-page-immunity-executive-health-{mobile,desktop}.json), so any clinical rewording must be applied in both places.

### /sleep-restoration-therapy/ — weak

Sleep disorders. The page covers insomnia and trouble falling asleep, broken sleep, sleep loss from stress, anxiety or overthinking, menopause-related sleep problems, jet lag, dependence on sleeping pills or sedatives, and daytime fatigue. It also lists sleep apnoea and PTSD, which is a clinical risk. Where it belongs in the sitemap, judged by what the page actually says: site-tree.json already puts it under 'Stress, sleep & headaches', next to /treatment-to-remove-stress-and-strain/, /yoga-based-stress-energy-reset-program/ and /treatment-for-sinusitis-and-migraine/. That placement is right. The page's own 'More programmes' rail is wrong: it points to Spine Care, Preventive Detox and Panchakarma. /faqs-downloads/ gives a recommended stay of 7 nights for sleep. Source files checked: prerender/sleep-restoration-therapy.html is the mobile view only and has no <head>. The FAQPage JSON-LD is in public/ahv/views/ahv-page-sleep-restoration-{desktop,mobile}.json. It has the same 5 questions as the visible FAQ, word for word, so every FAQ fix below must also be made in the JSON-LD. The desktop view text matches the mobile view.

FAQ questions on the page: 5.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the sleep restoration programme.” — The FAQ intro uses the banned word 'guests'. **Fix:** Here are the questions patients most often ask before starting the Sleep Restoration programme.
- [wrong-fact] “The World Health Organization reports traditional medicine in use across more than 170 of its 194 member states.” — The figure WHO usually cites is 170 of 194 member states, so 'more than 170' overstates it. The line also tells a sleep patient nothing about sleep. **Fix:** Check it against the WHO source. Either say '170 of its 194' and cite the source, or better, open with one line on what the FAQ covers.
- [overclaim-medical] “It functions by soothing the nervous system, harmonizing doshas (notably Vata), and rejuvenating the body’s inherent circadian rhythm.” — States a physiological mechanism as fact, including the effect on the nervous system and circadian rhythm. **Fix:** Put it in Ayurvedic terms ('In Ayurveda, sleep problems are often linked to aggravated Vata…') and send it to the physician for sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “At Kairali, we don’t treat the symptoms with short-term fixes.” — Suggests that conventional sleep medicine is a 'short-term fix'. This puts down prescribed treatment and may push patients on hypnotics to stop them. **Fix:** Your physician looks at the factors behind your sleep problem, such as stress, digestion and daily routine, alongside any treatment you already receive from your own doctor.
- [overclaim-medical] “assisting you in drifting off to sleep naturally and awakening fully revitalized.” — Promises an outcome ('fully revitalized'). **Fix:** Drop the promise, or soften it to 'to support more natural sleep', pending physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Visitors frequently describe enhancements like deeper, uninterrupted sleep, decreased mental noise, fewer stressors, increased energy” — 'Visitors' is the wrong register. 'Fewer stressors' makes no sense, since a therapy cannot remove outside stressors. The outcomes, including skin and digestion, are unsupported. **Fix:** Patients often report… Change 'fewer stressors' to 'less stress'. The whole answer needs physician sign-off, or it should be reframed as 'what the programme aims to support'.
- [overclaim-medical] “Over time, this treatment aids in resetting your body clock and restores you to a state of natural relaxation.” — Makes a definite outcome claim ('restores you'). **Fix:** Drop or soften it, e.g. 'aims to support a more regular sleep–wake rhythm'. Needs physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Ayurvedic sleep therapy to relieve insomnia, anxiety & stress.” — The hero line (also the meta description) claims to relieve anxiety, which is a psychiatric condition. **Fix:** An Ayurvedic programme for people living with insomnia and stress-related sleep problems. Needs physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Sleep Apnea or Shallow Breathing During Sleep” — Lists obstructive sleep apnoea as an indication. OSA needs a sleep-study diagnosis and usually CPAP. Offering an Ayurvedic programme for it could lead a patient to delay or drop CPAP. **Fix:** Remove it from 'Who is this for'. If the physician approves, replace it with a safety note: 'If you have diagnosed or suspected sleep apnoea, keep using your CPAP and tell the physician at consultation.'
- [overclaim-medical] “Post-Traumatic Stress or Emotional Imbalance” — Offers the programme for PTSD, a psychiatric disorder, with no word about working alongside psychiatric care. **Fix:** Remove it, or reword with the physician's approval (e.g. 'stress-related sleep disturbance, alongside your existing mental-health care').
- [overclaim-medical] “Dependency on Sleep Aids or Sedatives” — Offers a way off sedatives, and the Benefits list says 'Reduces Dependence on Sleep Medication'. Nothing says medication must be tapered under the prescribing doctor. Stopping benzodiazepines or Z-drugs abruptly is dangerous. **Fix:** Add a physician-approved line: 'Never stop or reduce prescribed sleep medication without your prescribing doctor; our physician will work with your current treatment.'
- [overclaim-medical] “awaken to complete wellness at Kairali’s renowned Healing Village.” — Promises an outcome ('complete wellness'). **Fix:** Delete the clause, or end with a factual line about the physician-led programme.
- [overclaim-medical] “We follow Ayurvedic principles using natural therapies and herbs to minimize side effects.” — Implies that natural means few side effects, which is a safety claim. **Fix:** Your physician selects therapies and herbal medicines for your constitution and monitors you throughout. Needs sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Ongoing support ensures long-term health and addresses any concerns or needs.” — 'Ensures long-term health' is a guarantee. **Fix:** Online follow-up consultations help you keep up the routine after discharge.
- [typo] “Treatment therapies include abhyangam, nasyam, shirodhara, takradhara, thapotichil, and sirovasti.Which will be administered as per body type” — 'thapotichil' is a typo for Thalapothichil. There is no space after 'sirovasti.', and 'Which will be…' is a sentence fragment. The names are lowercase here but capitalised elsewhere, and the spelling switches between Abhyangam and Abhyanga. **Fix:** Therapies may include Abhyangam, Nasyam, Shirodhara, Takradhara, Thalapothichil and Shirovasti, chosen by your physician for your constitution. Use one spelling throughout.
- [contradiction] “Daily Rejuvenation: One Ayurvedic therapy per day, including herbal steam baths to restore balance.” — Contradicts 'What We Offer: Daily 2 Therapies Per Person'. The routine also shows only one therapy slot (15:30). **Fix:** Owner to confirm the number of therapies per day, then use that one figure everywhere.
- [typo] “Daily Rejuvenation: Ayurvedic therapies per day, including herbal steam baths to restore balance.” — The number is missing ('… therapies per day'). The bullet is repeated under Mind-Body Practices with a different number. **Fix:** Keep one bullet, under Ayurvedic Therapies, with the confirmed number.
- [contradiction] “Evening Meditation: Wind down with private guided mindfulness sessions in the evening.” — The next bullet says 'Private sessions are also available at an additional cost', so the page says private sessions are both included and extra. **Fix:** Keep only the second bullet (group sessions included, private sessions extra), subject to owner confirmation.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — The same page says internal medicines and test reports 'will cost extra' and lists herbal medicines and lab tests as add-ons, so 'no hidden fees' is not true as written. /faqs-downloads/ also says herbal medicines during the stay are included. **Fix:** Owner to confirm whether medicines are included. Then write: 'Includes accommodation, meals, prescribed daily therapies and taxes; internal medicines and lab tests are charged as prescribed.'
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, but /faqs-downloads/ says airport and railway transfers cost extra and prices them (e.g. Coimbatore ₹6,775 one way). **Fix:** Airport transfers available on request (charged separately). Owner to confirm, and to confirm which languages the staff speak.
- [contradiction] “Price for Child between the Age of 5 to 12 years” — Lists a child extra bed at INR 3000, the same as the adult rate. /faqs-downloads/ says ages 5–12 pay 50% for food and extra bedding. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the child bedding rate and align both pages.
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — Expires in about five weeks. /faqs-downloads/ already quotes add-on rates valid from 1 Oct 2026 to 30 Sep 2027, so the two pages show overlapping validity windows. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the 2026–27 tariff before go-live.
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — 'Up to less than' is ungrammatical, and the 15–30-day band is unclear. **Fix:** 30 days or more before arrival: full refund. 15–29 days: 50% refund. Fewer than 15 days, or a change of dates during your stay: no refund. (Match the exact bands on /cancellation-and-refund-policy/.)
- [unclear] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk has no footnote. **Fix:** Add the footnote text, or remove the asterisk.
- [locked-word] “NABH Certified Hospital · 118+ Years Heritage · Individualised Programmes · World Luxury Award Winner” — Uses the banned word 'Luxury' (here and in the closing strip). It also says 'NABH Certified Hospital' while the NABH card says 'NABH-accredited for Panchakarma'. NABH accredits rather than certifies, and the scope differs. **Fix:** Use 'NABH-accredited' with the scope the owner confirms. Replace 'World Luxury Award Winner' with a non-banned credential.
- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Banned word 'Luxury'. **Fix:** Private, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility” — Banned word 'Luxury'. 'Stay' and 'unwind' are hospitality wording. **Fix:** Accommodation: private, eco-friendly villas for your admission
- [locked-word] “Premium luxury experience” — The Royal Villa tagline uses the banned word 'luxury'. **Fix:** Larger private villa. Owner to supply a factual descriptor.

- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Banned word 'journey'. **Fix:** …with your physician reviewing your progress throughout.

- [locked-word] “The serene surroundings and expert care made the journey incredibly soothing.” — Banned word 'journey' inside a patient testimonial. It is a quote, so the agent cannot edit it silently. **Fix:** Owner decides: use the quote without that sentence (with the patient's consent) or replace the testimonial.
- [locked-word] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — Typo: 'Worlds' should be 'World'. The page says nothing about contraindications or pre-admission screening, and does not say which 'recent reports' to bring._ Source: needs physician/owner. /faqs-downloads/ ('What special considerations do you have for senior patients?') covers medical monitoring and modified treatments in general terms only.
- How many nights do I need for insomnia, and when will I notice a difference in my sleep? — _The page only says 'Villas — Minimum Duration is 7 Nights'. It gives no recommended length for this condition and no realistic timeline, which are core booking inputs for someone flying long-haul._ Source: another site page for the length: /faqs-downloads/ says '7 nights — stress relief, sleep…'. Time to first effect: needs physician/owner.
- What actually happens each day on the sleep programme: which therapies, how long, and how many per day? — _The page contradicts itself (2 therapies per day vs 'One Ayurvedic therapy per day'), and the routine is the generic campus plan (Udwarthana 'to promote detoxification'). A patient cannot tell what they are paying for._ Source: needs physician/owner. A sleep-specific sample day and the confirmed number of therapies per day.
- What will the total cost be for 7–14 nights, including medicines, tests and consultations? Is the per-night villa tariff the full programme price? — _The tariff is per night per villa. Medicines and tests 'will cost extra' with no range, yet the page says 'All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees!' and /faqs-downloads/ says medicines during the stay are included. International patients will not pay 100% in advance for an unknown total._ Source: this page (per-night tariffs incl. taxes, add-ons) plus needs physician/owner (typical medicine and lab cost range; which statement on medicines is true)
- What happens if my sleep doesn't improve, or I relapse once I'm home? — _This is the central risk for a chronic insomniac spending thousands of euros. The page promises results but gives no expectation-setting, and the T&C allow no refund for a date change during the stay._ Source: needs physician/owner. Honest expectation-setting and the policy. /faqs-downloads/ gives online follow-ups at 1, 3 and 6 months.
- Who will treat me: qualifications, experience with sleep disorders, and do they speak English (or German, French, Russian, Arabic)? — _No physician is named on the page, and 'Doctor might change as subject to availability' adds doubt. 'Multilingual staff' does not say which languages the physicians speak._ Source: another site page: /online-consultation/ (BAMS physicians led by Chief Physician Dr. Deepu John) and /faqs-downloads/ (BAMS training). Languages and sleep-specific experience need physician/owner.
- Is there a modern-medicine doctor or hospital nearby if I have chest pain, a fall or a panic attack at night? — _Older patients with comorbidities, and a page that invites anxiety and PTSD cases, make emergency cover a deciding question._ Source: another site page: /faqs-downloads/ ('Emergency medical services on call'; arrangements with nearby hospitals; evacuation insurance advice). The hospital name and distance need the owner.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** 'Who is this for' lists 'Sleep Apnea or Shallow Breathing During Sleep' as an indication. OSA needs a sleep-study diagnosis and usually CPAP. Remove it or turn it into a safety note ('keep using your CPAP'). · 'Dependency on Sleep Aids or Sedatives' as an indication, together with the benefit 'Reduces Dependence on Sleep Medication'. This needs a physician-approved statement that prescribed hypnotics or sedatives are never stopped or reduced without the prescribing doctor, and that any taper is medically supervised. · 'Post-Traumatic Stress or Emotional Imbalance' as an indication, plus 'Promotes Emotional Healing'. These are psychiatric claims with no mention of working alongside psychiatric care. · Hero and meta description: 'Ayurvedic sleep therapy to relieve insomnia, anxiety & stress', and the benefit 'Reduces Stress, Anxiety, & Mental Restlessness'. These are claims to treat anxiety. · 'Sleep Disorders Related to Menopause or Hormonal Imbalance' plus the benefit 'Supports Hormonal Balance'. Endocrine claims, with nothing on HRT compatibility. · Benefits list outcome claims: 'Improves Quality and Duration of Sleep', 'Enhances Mental Clarity & Focus', 'Improves Digestion & Metabolism'. · FAQ 1 mechanism stated as fact: 'soothing the nervous system, harmonizing doshas (notably Vata), and rejuvenating the body’s inherent circadian rhythm'; also '…encourages profound, restorative sleep'. · FAQ 2: 'we don’t treat the symptoms with short-term fixes' puts down conventional sleep treatment, and 'drifting off to sleep naturally and awakening fully revitalized' is an outcome promise. · FAQ 3 results: 'deeper, uninterrupted sleep… improvements in skin and digestion' and 'restores you to a state of natural relaxation'. Unsubstantiated outcomes. · FAQ 4: 'sleep-enhancing herbal remedies' dispensed for home use. The efficacy claim and the safety of unsupervised home use, including interactions, need sign-off. · 'We follow Ayurvedic principles using natural therapies and herbs to minimize side effects'. A safety claim. · Diagnosis step: 'identify the root cause of your condition' by Nadi and Darshana Pariksha. The physician should decide whether the page must also say that medical causes of insomnia (thyroid, depression, OSA, restless legs, medication effects) are screened for or referred. · The Process therapy list (Abhyangam, Nasyam, Shirodhara, Takradhara, Thalapothichil, Shirovasti) and the routine's heat therapies ('herbal steam baths', 'warm herbal bath', Udwarthana 'to promote detoxification'). Confirm the sleep protocol and state contraindications for patients aged 40–70 with hypertension or cardiac disease. · 'Ongoing support ensures long-term health' (doctor-guided section) and 'awaken to complete wellness' (About). These are guarantees. · The 'Transformative Results' heading uses two Panchakarma testimonials, one citing 'better sleep', on a sleep-therapy page. This needs review under the rules on testimonials in Ayurvedic/AYUSH advertising, with patient consent confirmed. · FAQ intro WHO statistic ('more than 170 of its 194 member states'). Verify the figure and source before publishing, or remove it.

### /womens-hormonal-balance-endocrine-therapy/ — weak

The page is for women with hormonal or endocrine concerns. It lists PCOS/PCOD, thyroid imbalance (both hypothyroid and hyperthyroid), perimenopause and menopause, irregular cycles, hormonal acne, hormone-linked weight gain or sluggish metabolism, fatigue, mood changes (anxiety, irritability), recovery after hormonal therapy or contraceptive use, and fertility problems linked to hormones. It is a residential programme called Strī Swāsthyam Rakshana, with a minimum stay of 21 nights. From ₹22,780 per night for a Deluxe single, about ₹478,380 for 21 nights (matches long-form-programme-set.json). Care is doctor-guided: consultation, then Nadi and Darshana assessment, then Panchakarma procedures (Virechanam, Vasti, Matra Vasti), Abhyangam, Udwarthanam and Kizhi, plus herbal medicines, a sattvic diet, yoga and pranayama, and follow-up. Placement in the sitemap: site-tree.json and section-nav.json put this page under 'Women's health' next to Postpartum Rejuvenation, which matches its content. But the page content also overlaps with metabolic care (PCOS insulin resistance, thyroid, weight), sleep (menopausal insomnia) and stress/mood. Yet its 'More programmes' links go to Weight Management, Panchakarma and Complete Rejuvenation, and not to its content siblings: Postpartum (/post-pregnancy-health-programme/), Diabetes & Metabolic Balance (/treatment-for-diabetes/), Sleep Restoration (/sleep-restoration-therapy/) and Stress (/treatment-to-remove-stress-and-strain/).

FAQ questions on the page: 1.

**Proofreading (verified):**

It gives no conditions, no physician lead, no 21-night duration and no mention of Panchakarma. 'Usually' is vague, and 'detoxification' is the only clinical idea offered. **Fix:** Rewrite using only facts already on the page, e.g.: 'A physician-led residential Ayurvedic programme (minimum 21 nights) for women with hormonal and endocrine concerns such as PCOS, thyroid imbalance and menopausal symptoms. After a consultation and Ayurvedic assessment, your physician prescribes therapies (which may include Panchakarma procedures), herbal medicines, diet and daily yoga.' Needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [duplicate-question] “What is Women’s Hormonal Balance & Endocrine Therapy?” — The FAQ has one question, and it repeats the H1 and the About section. It answers none of the questions a patient has before booking. **Fix:** Replace it with 8–12 condition-specific questions: medication and herb interactions, who should not take the programme, reports to bring, day-by-day sequence, follow-up at home, and so on (see objectionGaps).
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the women’s hormonal balance & endocrine programme.” — Uses the banned word 'guests'. It also says 'questions' (plural) above a single question. **Fix:** 'Questions patients most often ask before admission to this programme.'
- [contradiction] “Transparent Add-Ons: Internal herbal medicines, lab tests, or specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed.” — Contradiction. The About and Process sections say the programme itself includes Panchakarma (Virechanam, Vasti, Matra Vasti), but this line lists Panchakarma as a paid add-on. It also contradicts /faqs-downloads/ q-18, which lists 'Herbal medicines and formulations during your stay' as included. **Fix:** Owner to state whether the Panchakarma procedures and internal medicines are included in this programme's fee, then make this page and /faqs-downloads/ q-18 say the same thing.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — Contradicted by the same page: 'Internal Medicines and test reports needed for your treatment can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses and will cost extra.' Laundry, phone and internet are also extra. A patient paying 100% in advance cannot budget for the cost of medicines and tests. **Fix:** Remove 'All-Inclusive / No hidden fees', or list exactly what is extra. If the owner can supply one, add an indicative range for medicines and tests (needs owner).
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, but /faqs-downloads/ q-18, q-24 and q-56 price transfers as extras (e.g., ₹6,775 one-way from Coimbatore Airport). The page also does not say which languages 'multilingual' covers. **Fix:** 'Airport transfers available at extra cost (see How to Reach)'. Name the languages staff speak only after the owner confirms them.
- [wrong-fact] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to September 30, 2026.” — These rates expire in 7 days (audit date 23 Sep 2026). /faqs-downloads/ already quotes add-on rates 'valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027'. **Fix:** Owner to supply the 2026–27 programme rates for Tier 5 (women's health) before 1 Oct 2026. Otherwise the page will show lapsed prices.
- [contradiction] “◆ NABH Certified Hospital” — The trust bar says 'NABH Certified Hospital', but the award card says 'NABH-accredited for Panchakarma — certificate AH-2024-0250'. The wording differs (certified vs accredited) and so does the scope (whole hospital vs Panchakarma). **Fix:** Use one wording that matches the certificate's actual scope. The owner confirms the scope from certificate AH-2024-0250.
- [overclaim-medical] “Rejuvenate your endocrine health naturally at Kairali’s healing retreat.” — The hero line, meta description and OG description all imply an outcome ('rejuvenate endocrine health') and use a holiday word ('retreat') for an NABH hospital programme. **Fix:** e.g., 'Physician-led Ayurvedic treatment for women with PCOS, thyroid imbalance and menopausal symptoms at Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village.' Needs sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “A sattvic diet, guided meditation, and lifestyle guidance additionally aid in endocrine revitalization.” — 'Endocrine revitalization' is an outcome claim with nothing to back it. **Fix:** '...support your treatment plan.' Needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “and natural rhythm—softly and entirely.” — 'Entirely' suggests complete restoration, which is an overclaim. The phrasing is also odd. **Fix:** Delete 'softly and entirely', e.g. '...emotional health and natural rhythm.'
- [overclaim-medical] “Transformative Results” — This heading claims outcomes. Both testimonials under it are general Panchakarma reviews from male patients, and they are reused on 8–9 other programme pages. None comes from a woman treated for a hormonal condition. **Fix:** Retitle it 'Patient experiences'. Add a verified, consented testimonial from a patient of this programme, or remove the section.
