- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Banned word "Luxury" in the programme's own inclusions copy. **Fix:** Private, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility, blending traditional charm with modern comforts.” — Banned word "Luxury" and holiday framing ("Unwind"). **Fix:** Accommodation: private, eco-friendly villas on the hospital campus, blending traditional Kerala design with modern comforts.
- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Banned word "Journey" in the accordion title. **Fix:** Inclusions — What Your Programme Includes
- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Banned word "journey". "Safe and effective" is also an outcome promise. **Fix:** with continued physician support throughout your treatment.
- [locked-word] “Daily wellness check-ins with your Ayurvedic doctor to tailor your journey.” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** Daily check-ins with your Ayurvedic physician to adjust your treatment.
- [locked-word] “Learn about the journey from seed to medicine.” — Banned word "journey" (Monday organic farm visit). **Fix:** See how a medicinal plant goes from seed to medicine.
- [locked-word] “Premium luxury experience” — Banned word "luxury" in the Royal Villa tagline, in the programme tariff table. **Fix:** Owner to supply a register-safe tagline, e.g. "Our most spacious villa" (verify the fact before using it).
- [locked-word] “Celebrated as a leading wellness and spa brand, Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village was honored at the South Asian Travel Awards 2018.” — Banned word "spa" in Kairali's own description text, not in an award's proper noun. **Fix:** Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village was honoured at the South Asian Travel Awards 2018.
- [locked-word] “Acknowledged for its outstanding service and guest satisfaction” — Banned word "guest" in Kairali's own copy. It appears twice (Certificate of Excellence 2018 and 2019). **Fix:** Acknowledged for its outstanding service and patient satisfaction
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — Typo. The award is "World Luxury Spa Awards", with no possessive "s". The image alt text has the same error. **Fix:** World Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024” — The heading says "Worlds" but the body says "World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024". **Fix:** World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The body spells it CONSIDICI; the heading spells it COSIDICI. **Fix:** was honoured with the COSIDICI National Award in 2019.
- [typo] “South Asian Travel Awards 2018.” — Stray full stop in the heading; no other award heading has one. **Fix:** South Asian Travel Awards 2018
- [wrong-fact] “Wellness Brand of the Year 2016” — Its "Read more" link goes to the AYUSH Brand of the Year 2015 article (whyayurveda.org/ayush-brand-of-the-year-2015-award-to-kairali-ayurvedic-group/), the same URL as the AYUSH 2015 card. Separately, the FIT Premium 2020 and 2024 cards both link to one article. **Fix:** Repoint to the correct 2016 article, or drop the link. Owner to supply the URL.
- [contradiction] “With over two decades of experience and a holistic approach, we emphasize personalized care and well-being.” — Contradicts the same page's "118+ Years Heritage" and "since 1908". A reader cannot tell which number applies to this hospital. **Fix:** Say what each number refers to (the family's practice since 1908 vs the hospital's years of operation). Owner to confirm the hospital's founding year.
- [grammar] “Individuals with nervous disabilities seek non-invasive therapies.” — Grammar: the list item became a sentence. The parallel items read "Those seeking…". **Fix:** Individuals with nervous disabilities seeking non-invasive therapies.
- [grammar] “we take a holistic Ayurvedic approach to address holistic nervous system healing.” — "Holistic" is used twice in one clause, and "address … healing" is not idiomatic. **Fix:** we take a holistic Ayurvedic approach to nervous-system health.
- [grammar] “Why one should opt for this therapy?” — Ungrammatical question heading. **Fix:** Why choose this therapy?
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Grammar, and it reads as a warning with no context. **Fix:** Your assigned physician may change, subject to availability.
- [typo] “MORE PROGRAMs” — Broken casing, and US spelling against the required "programme" register. **Fix:** More Programmes
- [typo] “Who is This Program For?” — Inconsistent title case, and "Program" where the register requires "programme". The same happens in "Program details", "Personalized Doctor-Guided Program", "Ayurvedic Program for Nerve Regeneration" and "Discover More Customizable Programs", while other parts of the page say "programme". **Fix:** Who Is This Programme For? Standardise on "programme" across the page.
- [unclear] “We prioritize empowering clients with a comprehensive understanding of their condition and treatment” — Register drift: "clients" instead of "patients" here and in "Clients are empowered" and "client comfort". **Fix:** We help every patient understand their condition and treatment
- [unclear] “Throughout your stay at our retreat, our team monitors your progress, adjusts the treatment plan if necessary” — Register drift: "retreat" instead of "hospital". It recurs in "after leaving the retreat", "fellow retreat participants", FAQ 2 "our retreat", and the footer "A serene retreat in India". Not a banned word, but it feeds the holiday perception. **Fix:** Throughout your admission, your physician monitors your progress and adjusts the treatment plan if necessary
- [wrong-fact] “At Kairali Wellness Retreats, our process for treating nervous disability through Ayurveda” — This image alt text uses a brand name that is not the entity ("Kairali Wellness Retreats"), plus retreat framing. **Fix:** Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village physician consultation for a neurological programme (describe the actual image)
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under Inclusions, which implies transfers are included. /faqs-downloads/ lists transfers as extra-cost services priced from ₹6,775 (Coimbatore, MPV). "Multilingual" names no languages. **Fix:** Airport transfers on request (charged separately — see How to Reach). Owner to name the languages actually spoken.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — Contradicts the same page: internal medicines and test reports "will cost extra", and extra treatment is billed "As per Doctor’s Prescription". For a neuro patient, medicines are core to treatment. **Fix:** Programme rate includes accommodation, meals, the prescribed daily therapies and taxes. Internal medicines, lab tests and additional therapies are prescribed and charged separately. Ask for an estimate. (Owner to supply a typical range.)
- [unclear] “(Note: Preventive Health & Regenerative Program includes one therapy per day.)” — A note about a different programme, pasted into this page's inclusions. It confuses the reader. **Fix:** Remove from this page.
- [unclear] “Transparent Add-Ons: Internal herbal medicines, lab tests, or specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed.” — About names Shirodhara, Shirovasti, Nasyam, Pizhichil and Pichu (Nasyam is a Panchakarma procedure). The patient cannot tell which fall within the "2 therapies per day" and which are paid add-ons. **Fix:** Physician or owner to state which of the named neuro therapies are within the daily inclusion.
- [unclear] “Engage in an Ayurvedic therapy session, such as Abhyanga (Ayurvedic massage) or Udwarthana (herbal powder massage), to promote detoxification and relaxation.” — A generic day plan. Udwarthana is a drying, Kapha-reducing therapy, an odd example for a Vata/neurological programme. The page's own neuro therapies (Shirodhara, Shirovasti, Nasyam, Pizhichil) are missing from the sample day. **Fix:** Dr. Deepu John to supply a neurological-programme sample day.
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — The rates expire about 5 weeks from today (23 Sep 2026). /faqs-downloads/ quotes the same add-on figures (extra bed ₹3,000 / $43 / €38) as "valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027". The two validity windows conflict. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the current tariff and a single validity window across both pages.
- [unclear] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk points to no footnote. **Fix:** Remove the asterisk or add the footnote it refers to.
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — "Up to less than" is ungrammatical, and "Up to 30 days before" is ambiguous. /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ says "30 days or more" and "between 15 days and 30 days". **Fix:** 30 days or more before arrival – full refund. 15–29 days – 50% refund. Less than 15 days, or a change of dates during the stay – no refund.
- [grammar] “which included dietary adjustments, meditation, and little exercise.” — "Little exercise" means almost none; "light exercise" is almost certainly intended. It sits in a patient quote. **Fix:** If the quote is not verbatim from the patient, correct it to "light exercise". If it is verbatim, use "[light] exercise".
- [wrong-fact] “NABH Certified Hospital” — NABH grants accreditation, not certification. The same page states the scope precisely: "NABH-accredited for Panchakarma — certificate AH-2024-0250". Used twice. **Fix:** NABH-accredited (Panchakarma)
- [unclear] “Explore our certified organic farm” — "Certified organic" is a factual claim with no certifying body named. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the certification and the body, or drop "certified".
- [grammar] “Bring: Cotton clothes, medications, recent reports & open mind” — Grammar ("an open mind"). For a neuro patient, "recent reports" is too vague. **Fix:** Bring: cotton clothes, all current medicines with prescriptions, recent medical reports (e.g. MRI, nerve studies, neurologist letters) and an open mind. Physician to confirm the report list.
- [typo] “₹ INR $ USD € EURO” — Currency code inconsistency: the code is EUR, and the add-ons block says "Euro". **Fix:** ₹ INR  $ USD  € EUR
- [unclear] “Experience Ayurvedic therapies for nervous system balance, stress relief, and cognitive wellness with doctor-guided care at Kairali Healing Village.” — The hero and meta description sell stress relief and cognitive wellness, while the slug, About and testimonial address neurological disease. No specific condition is named anywhere, so a patient with Parkinson's, MS, neuropathy or post-stroke deficits cannot tell whether the page applies to them. The brand is also shortened to "Kairali Healing Village". **Fix:** Physician or owner to name the conditions treated and not treated, and align the hero with that scope. Use the full entity name.
- [typo] “(Kairali’s Treatment for Nervous Disability )” — The og:title metadata has a stray space before ")" and a double space before "Majja". It also does not match the H1. **Fix:** Neurological Wellness & Function Therapy (Majjā Poshanam Chikitsa) | Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 23 gaps):**
- Do you treat my specific diagnosis (Parkinson's, multiple sclerosis, diabetic or peripheral neuropathy, post-stroke weakness, Bell's palsy, early dementia, epilepsy), and which conditions do you decline? — _The page says only "nervous disability" and "nervous system imbalances". Nobody flies from Europe or the Gulf without knowing their disease is in scope._ Source: needs physician/owner. Related pages: /treatment-for-paralysis/ and /treatment-for-facial-paralysis/ cover subsets. /online-consultation/ promises the physician will say plainly if a programme is not appropriate.
- Can I keep taking my neurological medicines (levodopa, anti-epileptics, MS disease-modifying therapy, blood thinners, antidepressants), and will anyone tell me to stop them? — _This is the top safety fear for neuro patients and their neurologists. FAQ 3 dodges it with "ensuring compatibility"._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John)
- Do the herbal-mineral medicines (bhasma/rasaushadhi) contain heavy metals? Are they tested, and can I get an ingredient list to show my neurologist? — _Western patients have read about heavy metals in Ayurvedic preparations. FAQ 2's "natural herbs and minerals" raises the worry instead of settling it._ Source: needs physician/owner. /online-consultation/ mentions the group's WHO-GMP pharmacy, but no testing or ingredient-disclosure fact is stated.
- What result can I realistically expect, and by when: symptom relief, slower progression, or possibly nothing for a progressive disease? What happens if it does not help? — _The page promises "lasting results" and "transformative results" with no timeframe or honest limits. Older patients with chronic neuro disease distrust that._ Source: needs physician/owner. /online-consultation/ has usable honest-limits wording ("sometimes that view is that our programmes are not appropriate").
- How many nights do I need for a neurological condition, and is 7 nights enough? — _The page gives only "Villas — Minimum Duration is 7 Nights". Sister pages differ: /treatment-for-paralysis/ says 14 nights minimum and 14–28 days typical. International flights make this a key planning fact._ Source: another page (/faqs-downloads/ says neurological chronic-condition support runs "7 to 14 nights depending on the condition"). The recommended length for this programme needs physician/owner.
- I am 68 with heart disease, diabetes and high blood pressure alongside my neurological condition. Am I medically suitable, and are therapies like Shirodhara, Nasyam or Pizhichil safe for me? — _Comorbidities are the norm at 40–70. Nothing on the page addresses age or co-existing conditions._ Source: another page (/faqs-downloads/: no stated maximum age, "Modified treatments suitable for senior patients"). Individual suitability and contraindications need physician/owner.
- Who will treat me (name, qualifications, neurological experience), and do they speak English, German, French, Russian or Arabic? — _The page says "Our specialists" and "multilingual staff" but names nobody and no languages. It even warns "Doctor might change"._ Source: another page (/online-consultation/: Chief Physician Dr. Deepu John, BAMS; /faqs-downloads/: BAMS-qualified physicians). Languages spoken and any neuro specialisation need physician/owner.
- I have poor balance or use a wheelchair or walker. Are the villa, bathroom and treatment rooms accessible, and how do you prevent falls on oiled treatment tables and floors? — _Mobility impairment is common in this group. Oil therapies (Pizhichil, Abhyanga) are a real slip risk._ Source: another page (/how-to-reach/: wheelchair-accessible vehicle and caregiver on request; /faqs-downloads/: ramps, mobility assistance). Villa-level accessibility and fall precautions need physician/owner.
- What happens if I have a seizure, a fall or stroke symptoms during my stay? Is a doctor on campus at night, and which emergency hospital is nearest and how far? — _Neuro patients and their families need an emergency plan before booking a remote rural campus._ Source: another page (/faqs-downloads/: "arrangements with nearby hospitals and emergency services", "Emergency medical services on call"). The named hospital, distance and night cover need physician/owner.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** FAQ 1: "Yes, Ayurvedic treatment … can help alleviate nervous disabilities". An unqualified efficacy "Yes" across all neurological conditions. · FAQ 2: "Yes … made from natural herbs and minerals, minimizing the risk of adverse effects". An unqualified safety claim; the mineral preparations are not addressed. · FAQ 3: "ensuring compatibility and safety" when combined with other medicines. A guarantee, with no instruction not to stop prescribed neuro medication. · About: "an Ayurvedic Program for Nerve Regeneration". The page claims nerve regeneration. · About: "Ayurveda … offers an effective approach to addressing these issues", said of neurological disorders. · About: neurological disorders are "often caused by imbalanced Vata dosha". A causal claim that needs the physician's framing as Ayurvedic understanding. · About: "Ayurvedic diagnostics provide accurate insights". · Approach: "Customized medications from natural herbs and minerals aim to restore balance and strengthen the nervous system", and "lasting results". · Who it's for: "natural remedies for cognitive impairments" and "long-lasting results for nervous system health". This implies dementia or cognitive-decline benefit. · Why opt: "enhance cognitive function, and strengthen the nervous system for lasting well-being" and "prioritizing safety and effectiveness by blending Ayurvedic wisdom with modern expertise". · Why opt: "Our specialists focus on nervous disability treatment". Is there a neuro-specialised physician to back this? · Benefits list stated as outcomes: "reduced symptoms of stress and anxiety", "Improved cognitive function", "Strengthened nervous system resilience, restored balance, and increased vitality". · Post Treatment: "Ongoing support ensures long-term health", and "Continuous post-discharge wellness plan". Guarantee language. · Testimonials: the "Transformative Results" heading plus the single unverified testimonial "My time at Kairali changed my life … I was physically and mentally stronger" about a "neurological disease". Needs verification, consent and outcome-claim review. · Therapy list (Shirodhara, Shirovasti, Nasyam, Pizhichil, Pichu, Abhyanga) given as treatment for neurological disorders with no indications or contraindications, e.g. in epilepsy, uncontrolled hypertension or recent stroke. · Generic day plan offered for neuro patients: Udwarthana "to promote detoxification", 30–45 minute yoga asanas, nature walks, and a "detoxifying drink to kickstart the metabolism". Suitability for patients with mobility or balance impairment and the detox or metabolism claims need sign-off. · "Personalized Diet" / "Three organic, sattvic meals": any claimed therapeutic effect of the diet on neurological conditions needs sign-off before it is expanded.

### /revitalizing-and-strengthening/ — weak

Supportive (complementary) Ayurvedic care for adults having chemotherapy or recovering from it (the page lists only "Patients recovering from chemotherapy" and "Patients undergoing chemotherapy for cancer"). The aim is to help with side effects, strength, stress and quality of life. FAQ 2 says it does not replace conventional cancer treatment. The page also calls it Karkaroga Sahayam Chikitsa, a "cancer recovery program" (title and hero) and "Revitalizing and Strengthening Treatment for Cancer" (og:title).

FAQ questions on the page: 5.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [overclaim-medical] “Boost immunity, reduce side effects & regain strength with Kairali’s Ayurvedic cancer recovery program in Kerala.” — The hero, meta description and og:description call this a "cancer recovery program". That says patients recover from cancer, which the page cannot back up. The <title> says the same: "Ayurvedic Cancer Recovery & Strengthening Program | Kairali". "Boost immunity" and "reduce side effects" are also outcome claims. "program" should be "programme" for the KTAHV register. **Fix:** Use supportive-care wording, for example "Physician-led Ayurvedic supportive care alongside your oncology treatment, aimed at comfort, strength and quality of life". Apply it to the H1, <title>, meta and OG tags. Any outcome wording needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “support conventional medical therapy by lowering symptoms, managing side effects, and promoting a full recovery.” — In FAQ 2, "promoting a full recovery" reads as a promise of recovery from cancer. That is the biggest claim risk on the page, and it sits in the answer that should build trust. **Fix:** Remove "promoting a full recovery". Say that it may help with some treatment side effects and quality of life, and that it does not treat cancer and never replaces oncology care. Wording to be signed off by Dr. Deepu John.
- [overclaim-medical] “Are Kairali’s cancer treatments a replacement for conventional therapies?” — The questions keep describing Kairali as offering "cancer treatments" (Q1 "What type of cancer treatments does Kairali offer?", Q2) and "cancer care" (Q5). That frames an Ayurvedic hospital as an oncology provider, which conflicts with the answer to this very question. **Fix:** Reword to "Does Kairali's supportive care replace my oncology treatment?". Rename Q1 to "What does Kairali offer people having or recovering from cancer treatment?" and Q5 to "Why choose Kairali for supportive care?".
- [contradiction] “comprehensive Ayurvedic treatments that supplement traditional cancer treatments.” — On an Ayurveda page, "traditional" normally means Ayurveda itself, so "traditional cancer treatments" is confusing. FAQ 2 uses "conventional" for the same thing. The answer also never says what "type" of treatment is offered, which is what Q1 asks. "comprehensive" overstates it. **Fix:** Say "supportive Ayurvedic therapies alongside conventional (oncology) treatment" and list the therapies actually used for these patients. That list needs physician confirmation.
- [overclaim-medical] “assist the body’s natural healing processes by aiding in immune system strengthening, stress reduction, energy enhancement, and detoxification.” — FAQ 3 makes "immune system strengthening" and "detoxification" claims for cancer patients. For people on immunotherapy, targeted therapy or active chemotherapy these claims are clinically sensitive. **Fix:** Needs physician sign-off. Limit it to supportive aims such as fatigue, stress, sleep and appetite, with the words "may help", unless Dr. Deepu John approves more.
- [grammar] “tailored to each patient’s health status, cancer types, and body constitution.” — Grammar: one patient has one cancer type. It also implies protocols for each cancer type that the page never describes. **Fix:** "tailored to each patient's health, type of cancer, stage of treatment and constitution". Which cancer types are accepted needs the physician.
- [duplicate-question] “What are the key benefits of choosing Kairali for cancer care?” — The question asks why Kairali, but the answer lists generic benefits that repeat FAQ 3 ("vitality", "resilience") and never gives a reason to choose Kairali. FAQs 1, 3 and 5 all end up answering "what does it do". **Fix:** Answer with facts stated elsewhere on the site: NABH-accredited Ayurvedic hospital (certificate AH-2024-0250), BAMS-qualified physicians led by Chief Physician Dr. Deepu John, residential care. Drop the repeated benefit list.
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the supportive care therapy for cancer patients programme.” — Uses the banned word "guests". The programme name is also written in lower case in the middle of the sentence. **Fix:** "Here are the questions patients most often ask before starting the Supportive Care Therapy for Cancer Patients programme."
- [duplicate-question] “History of Supportive Care Therapy for Cancer Patients” — The History section repeats the About section word for word ("Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village has a long history of offering comprehensive care..."). It gives no history. **Fix:** Replace it with the real history of this programme at KTAHV (needs owner), or remove the section and its jump link.
- [contradiction] “One Ayurvedic therapy per day, including herbal steam baths to restore balance.” — The package box says "Daily 2 Therapies Per Person", but Inclusions says one therapy per day. That one-therapy line also sits by mistake under "Mind-Body Practices". **Fix:** Owner to confirm the correct daily therapy count for this programme and state it once.
- [typo] “Daily Rejuvenation: Ayurvedic therapies per day, including herbal steam baths to restore balance.” — The number of therapies is missing ("___ Ayurvedic therapies per day"). **Fix:** Insert the confirmed number, the same one used in the package box.
- [contradiction] “Evening Meditation: Wind down with private guided mindfulness sessions in the evening.” — "Evening Meditation" appears twice. The first entry says private sessions are included; the second says "Private sessions are also available at an additional cost." **Fix:** Keep one entry: group guided meditation included, private sessions at extra cost (owner to confirm).
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — The Terms on the same page contradict it: "Internal Medicines and test reports ... will cost extra", Panchakarma is an add-on, and laundry, internet and extra food are charged. For a cancer patient, medicines and blood tests are likely to be a significant cost. **Fix:** Remove "All-Inclusive" and "No hidden fees!". List plainly what is included and what is charged extra.
- [contradiction] “Internal herbal medicines, lab tests, or specialty therapies (e.g., Panchakarma) available as needed.” — About says the programme uses "individualized therapies including Panchakarma, herbal formulations", and Process step 04 presents Panchakarma as core. Inclusions calls Panchakarma and herbal medicines add-ons. The patient cannot tell what the price covers. **Fix:** Owner to state whether Panchakarma and internal medicines are part of this package or chargeable, then align About, Process and Inclusions.
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under package Inclusions, but /faqs-downloads/ says airport transfers are "at extra cost" (Coimbatore ₹6,775 MPV / ₹23,711 sedan, one way). The languages covered by "multilingual" are not stated. **Fix:** "Airport transfers can be arranged at extra cost" with a link to /how-to-reach/. Name the languages actually spoken (needs owner).
- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Banned word "luxury". **Fix:** "Private, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities"
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility” — Banned word "luxury", plus holiday-style framing on a cancer page. **Fix:** "Accommodation: a private, eco-friendly villa for the length of your admission"
- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "Inclusions — what your programme includes"
- [locked-word] “Daily wellness check-ins with your Ayurvedic doctor to tailor your journey.” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "Daily check-ins with your Ayurvedic physician to adjust your treatment."
- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Banned word "journey". "safe and effective" is also a safety and efficacy promise. **Fix:** "with physician supervision throughout your admission."
- [locked-word] “Learn about the journey from seed to medicine.” — Banned word "journey". **Fix:** "Learn how a medicinal plant becomes a medicine."
- [locked-word] “Premium luxury experience” — Banned word "luxury" in the Royal Villa tagline. **Fix:** "Our most spacious villa" or similar. The owner confirms the factual difference between villas.
- [locked-word] “Transformative Panchakarma Journey at Kairali” — The testimonial heading, written by the site, uses the banned word "journey". The review texts also say "journey" twice; those are patients' own words. **Fix:** Retitle to "Panchakarma at Kairali". Owner to decide whether to keep verbatim review wording or choose other reviews.
- [overclaim-medical] “Transformative Results” — On a cancer page this H2 implies treatment outcomes. None of the three testimonials under it is from a cancer patient; all are generic Panchakarma reviews. **Fix:** Use testimonials from patients in this programme, if they exist and have consent (needs owner), with a neutral heading such as "What patients say". Otherwise remove the section here.
- [locked-word] “Celebrated as a leading wellness and spa brand” — Descriptive copy, not an award's proper name, using the banned word "spa". **Fix:** "Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village was honoured at the South Asian Travel Awards 2018."
- [locked-word] “Acknowledged for its outstanding service and guest satisfaction” — Banned word "guest" in descriptive copy (appears twice, on Certificate of Excellence 2018 and 2019). **Fix:** "patient satisfaction"
- [locked-word] “Kairali therapist tending a reclining guest with eye compresses during a shirodhara oil-pour therapy” — Banned word "guest" in an aria-label. Screen readers and crawlers read it even though sighted visitors do not see it. **Fix:** "reclining patient"
- [locked-word] “socializing with fellow retreat participants” — Not a banned word but off the clinical register. The same happens in "settle into your serene retreat" and the footer's "A serene retreat ... for overall well-being and relaxation". **Fix:** "fellow patients". Replace "retreat" with "the hospital campus".
- [wrong-fact] “NABH Certified Hospital” — NABH accredits; it does not certify. The same page says "NABH-accredited for Panchakarma — certificate AH-2024-0250", so the chip may overstate the scope. **Fix:** "NABH-accredited". Owner to confirm whether to say "hospital" or "for Panchakarma".
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — Misspells the award name. It is "World Luxury Spa Awards", as in the other captions. The heading "Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024" has the same error while its body says "World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024". **Fix:** "World Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024" / "World Luxury Hotel Awards 2024"
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The body says CONSIDICI; the heading and alt text say COSIDICI. **Fix:** "COSIDICI National Award"
- [typo] “South Asian Travel Awards 2018.” — Stray full stop in the award heading, alt text and aria-label. **Fix:** "South Asian Travel Awards 2018"
- [grammar] “Who is This Program For?” — The page mixes "Program" (About the Program, Personalized Doctor-Guided Program, Pre-Program, Customizable Programs, "programs" in FAQ 4) with "Programmes". The required KTAHV register is "programme". "This" is capitalised mid-sentence. **Fix:** Use "programme" everywhere: "Who is this programme for?"
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Ungrammatical. **Fix:** "Your physician may change, subject to availability."
- [unclear] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — "Up to less than" is contradictory. "Up to 30 days before the arrival date – Full Refund" literally means cancelling within 30 days gets a full refund, which is the opposite of the policy. /faqs-downloads/ states it clearly. **Fix:** Use the /faqs-downloads/ wording: full refund 30 or more days before admission; 50% refund 15–30 days before; no refund less than 15 days before or for a date change during the stay.
- [contradiction] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — These rates expire about five weeks from today (23 Sep 2026). /faqs-downloads/ already quotes add-on rates "valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027", so the two validity windows overlap and conflict. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the current rate card and validity dates, then align both pages.
- [contradiction] “Extra Bed INR 3000” — The child (5–12) extra bed is priced the same as the adult extra bed (INR 3,000). /faqs-downloads/ says ages 5–12 are "charged 50% for food and extra bedding". EUR/USD child rates are also missing. **Fix:** Owner to confirm the child rate and align it with /faqs-downloads/.
- [unclear] “The above rates are inclusive of applicable taxes*.” — The asterisk has no footnote. The tax statement is repeated in the Terms ("All rates and packages are inclusive of all taxes."). The tariff also never says whether "Single ₹21,567 per night" is per villa or per person. **Fix:** Remove the asterisk or add its footnote. State the pricing basis (per villa per night, occupancy) next to the 14-night minimum.

Protein intake is a real concern for patients losing weight during chemotherapy. **Fix:** "...and plant proteins such as lentils and pulses". Any protein adaptation for cancer patients needs the physician.

It does not address someone who already has cancer. **Fix:** Rewrite the "Why choose" and "Benefits" lists for this condition: comfort during and after chemotherapy, strength, sleep, appetite, emotional support. Wording needs physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “By strengthening the immune system, it reduces the risk of illnesses and boosts the body’s defence mechanisms.” — A disease-risk and immunity claim addressed to cancer patients. **Fix:** Remove, or reword for Dr. Deepu John's sign-off.

- [overclaim-medical] “maintain optimal health and prevent diseases naturally.” — A disease-prevention claim in the Sunday session copy, on a cancer page. **Fix:** "...to support your day-to-day health."

Fix the duplicate route's title and description.

**Must-answer questions missing (13 of 26 gaps):**
- Is it safe to take your herbal medicines and have oil therapies while I am on chemotherapy, immunotherapy, targeted therapy or hormone therapy (e.g. tamoxifen, aromatase inhibitors)? Will they interact with my cancer drugs? — _Herb–drug interaction is the first thing an oncologist asks and the most common reason patients do not book. The page promises "herbal formulations" and "immune system strengthening" but never mentions interactions._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John): which herbs are avoided with which drug classes and how interactions are screened
- Should I stop or delay any of my oncology treatment to come? — _FAQ 2 says the programme is not a replacement, but no answer says "never stop or delay oncology treatment". A patient deciding on a 14-night minimum stay needs that said plainly._ Source: /disclaimer/ §6 already says patients should maintain all ongoing treatment and follow their own physicians' advice; confirm cancer-specific wording with the physician
- Do you need my oncologist's written approval, and which reports must I send before booking (latest blood counts, staging, scans, treatment summary, drug list)? — _The page only says "Bring: ... recent reports & open mind". Patients having chemotherapy need to know whether they will be accepted before paying 100% in advance._ Source: needs physician/owner (the free online consultation on /online-consultation/ may be where reports are reviewed; confirm)
- Who is NOT suitable: low blood counts, metastatic disease, recent surgery, a port/PICC line, lymphoedema, bone metastases, radiotherapy skin reactions? — _"Who is this programme for" lists only two lines and no exclusions. Massage, heat, steam baths and purgation all carry specific risks for these patients._ Source: needs physician/owner
- When in my treatment should I come: between cycles, after the last cycle, how long after surgery or radiotherapy? Can the 14-night minimum fit between chemotherapy cycles? — _The page targets people "undergoing chemotherapy" but requires "Minimum Duration is 14 Nights", which usually clashes with a 2–3-week cycle._ Source: needs physician/owner (the 14-night minimum is on this page; timing and exceptions need the physician)
- Which therapies will I actually receive: Panchakarma purgation, Abhyanga, Shirodhara, Udwarthana, herbal steam? Are they changed for cancer patients? — _The daily routine is generic, e.g. "Udwarthana ... to promote detoxification" and a warm herbal bath. Patients with neuropathy, fatigue or fragile skin need to know the therapies are adapted._ Source: needs physician/owner
- What happens if I get a fever, infection or other complication during my stay? Which hospital with oncology or emergency care is nearest, how far is it, and is a doctor on site at night? — _Neutropenic fever is a medical emergency for chemotherapy patients far from their own oncology team._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ says there are "arrangements with nearby hospitals and emergency services" and recommends evacuation insurance; the hospital name, distance and night cover need owner
- Is it safe for an immunocompromised patient to take part in group yoga, the pool, the organic farm visit and the soil/composting class? — _The weekly schedule includes a farm visit and a class on "Soil health and composting". Soil and shared-water exposure is an infection risk during chemotherapy, and infection control is not mentioned._ Source: needs physician/owner (infection-control policy, which activities are physician-cleared)
- Does the physician have experience with cancer patients? Will they talk to my oncologist and give me notes to take home? — _Patients aged 40–70 trust their oncologist first and need to know the Ayurvedic physician will work with them, not against them._ Source: /online-consultation/ names Dr. Deepu John (Chief Physician, BAMS), Dr. Akhila Oommen and Dr. Rahul R; /faqs-downloads/ confirms discharge notes and treatment summaries for your home doctor; oncology experience needs physician/owner
- What can I realistically expect to improve (fatigue, appetite, nausea, sleep, neuropathy, mood), how soon, and how is progress measured? — _The page offers "Transformative Results" but no condition-specific, honest expectations. Overpromising here is both a trust risk and a clinical risk._ Source: needs physician/owner (no outcome may be invented; /disclaimer/ §7 already states no results are guaranteed)
- Is this price per villa or per person, what will 14 nights cost in total, and roughly how much should I budget for medicines and blood tests? — _The tariff shows per-night figures without a basis. The page says "All-Inclusive" yet lists medicines, lab tests and Panchakarma as extras, which may be a large part of the cost for this condition._ Source: needs owner (rate basis and a typical medicine/lab cost range)
- If my oncologist postpones my cycle or I am too unwell to fly, do I lose my 100% advance? — _Cancer schedules change often. The page requires 100% advance and gives no refund under 15 days, with nothing about medical postponement._ Source: needs owner (medical-postponement policy); the general policy is on /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ and /faqs-downloads/
- Are airport transfers included, and how far is the hospital from the airport? — _This page lists airport transfers under Inclusions, but /faqs-downloads/ says they cost extra. The two pages also disagree on distance: Coimbatore is 75 km on /faqs-downloads/ and ~62 km on /how-to-reach/; Kochi is 125 km vs ~115 km._ Source: /how-to-reach/ and /faqs-downloads/ (transfer prices); owner must reconcile the distances and the inclusion

**For Dr. Deepu John:** Title, H1, meta and OG framing: "Ayurvedic Cancer Recovery & Strengthening Program" and "Boost immunity, reduce side effects & regain strength with Kairali’s Ayurvedic cancer recovery program". This implies recovery from cancer and states outcomes. · FAQ 2: "...lowering symptoms, managing side effects, and promoting a full recovery." This implies full recovery from cancer. · FAQ 1: "boosting immunity, minimizing side effects". FAQ 3: "immune system strengthening, stress reduction, energy enhancement, and detoxification". These are immunity and detox claims to cancer patients, including those on immunotherapy. · FAQ 4: treatments tailored to "cancer types". This implies cancer-type-specific protocols. The physician must confirm which cancer types and treatment stages are accepted or excluded. · About: "Cancer patients’ physical and mental problems are addressed by these treatments", "enhancing immunity, reviving vitality" and "has a long history of offering comprehensive care" for cancer patients. Efficacy and track-record claims. · Who it is for: "Patients undergoing chemotherapy for cancer". Whether residential therapy during active chemotherapy is suitable, and on what clearance, needs physician sign-off. · Process step 04: "Panchakarma, a comprehensive detoxification process, helps eliminate toxins from the body", presented as standard for these patients. · Why choose / Benefits: "By strengthening the immune system, it reduces the risk of illnesses", "It focuses on preventing health issues before they arise", "prevents potential health issues" and "supporting long-term wellness and longevity". Longevity implies survival benefit. · "Transformative Results" H2 over three generic Panchakarma testimonials on a cancer page. Implies outcomes this programme may not deliver. · Daily routine and inclusions given to chemotherapy patients without adaptation: "Udwarthana (herbal powder massage), to promote detoxification", "herbal steam baths", "a warm herbal bath", "detoxifying drink to kickstart the metabolism", 30–45 min yoga asanas, daily group yoga, nature walks. Intensity and suitability need sign-off. · Organic farm visit and the "Soil health and composting" agriculture class, offered with no infection-control note to possibly immunocompromised patients. · Sunday session: "practical do’s and don’ts to maintain optimal health and prevent diseases naturally". A disease-prevention claim. · During Treatment: "continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey". A safety and efficacy promise. · Follow-up: "The practitioner may suggest herbal supplements" and "Continuous post-discharge wellness plan". Herbal medicines continued at home alongside oncology drugs need an interaction statement. · Missing safety statement: the page never tells patients to keep their oncology treatment, or that herbs are screened against their cancer drugs. /disclaimer/ §6 has general wording; a cancer-specific version needs physician sign-off. · Classical programme name "Karkaroga Sahayam Chikitsa": the physician should confirm the correct term and spelling (Karkataroga?).

### /treatment-for-slip-disc/ — weak

Slip disc: a herniated or prolapsed disc in the lower back or neck, with nerve symptoms such as back or neck pain, pain running down an arm or leg (sciatica), numbness, tingling and weakness. The programme is called "Spinal Disc & Nerve Restoration Treatment" (Snayu Bala Chikitsa). It is residential, offered as 14, 21 or 28 days, with a 14-night villa minimum. The page states no clinical outcome data.

FAQ questions on the page: 3.

- [unclear] “occurs when the soft cushioning disc between the spinal vertebrae ruptures or bulges, causing pain, numbness, and tingling sensations.” — Imprecise. It is the disc's soft centre pushing through a tear, and symptoms can occur rather than always occurring. It does not mention the neck vs lower back or sciatica. **Fix:** (Physician to approve.) A slip (herniated) disc is when the soft centre of a spinal disc pushes through a tear in its outer ring. If it presses on a nerve it can cause back or neck pain, pain down an arm or leg (sciatica), numbness, tingling or weakness.
- [unclear] “Suffering from numbness or discomfort? Weakness or injury often leads to this issue, with factors like age, smoking, and obesity playing a role.” — "This issue" is never named. "Weakness" is ambiguous, and muscle weakness is also a red-flag symptom. **Fix:** (Physician to approve.) A slip disc can cause back or neck pain, pain running down an arm or leg, numbness or tingling. Age-related wear, injury, smoking and excess weight are common contributing factors.

When is surgery or urgent care the right answer instead, for example loss of bladder or bowel control, saddle numbness, worsening leg weakness or foot drop, or uncontrolled pain? — _The page implies patients can avoid invasive procedures and lists no exclusions. - I take painkillers, NSAIDs, pregabalin/gabapentin, muscle relaxants or blood thinners, plus BP or diabetes medicines. Do I keep taking them, and will your herbal medicines interact with them? — _FAQ 3 only says to disclose your medications, while the Benefits section promises reduced pain medication. - Can oil massage, Pizhichil, Elakizhi or hot Kati Vasti make a herniated disc or nerve pain worse, especially during an acute flare? Will anyone manipulate my spine? — _Massage and heat on an injured disc is the first fear of patients whose spine surgeon or physio told them to avoid manipulation._ Source: needs physician/owner
- I am 60–70 with diabetes, hypertension, osteoporosis or a heart condition, or I have already had spine surgery (discectomy, fusion, implants). Can I still do this programme? — _Comorbidities and previous surgery are common in this age band and decide eligibility. The page does not address them._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ states there is no maximum age and lists senior-patient considerations. Eligibility with comorbidities or after surgery: needs physician/owner.
- How soon will I feel a difference? Is 14 nights enough, or will I need 21 or 28? How is progress measured? — _The page offers 14/21/28 days with no guidance on which one fits whom. Duration drives both flight and budget decisions._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ says musculoskeletal programmes start from 14 nights, and this page states a 14-night minimum. Condition-specific timeline and progress measures: needs physician/owner.
- What will a slip-disc patient's day actually look like? Which therapies on which days, how long on the treatment table, and is yoga or the nature walk compulsory? — _The routine shown is generic: 30–45 min yoga asanas at 06:30, a nature walk, and Udwarthana "to promote detoxification". It does not fit a patient in acute nerve pain._ Source: needs physician/owner (a condition-specific sample day or week)
- What will the whole stay cost for 14, 21 or 28 nights once herbal medicines, lab tests and any extra therapies (e.g. Panchakarma/Vasti) are added? — _The page gives per-night villa rates and says medicines and tests are extra, "ascertained as your treatment progresses". With 100% payment in advance, an open-ended bill is a major barrier._ Source: This page has the per-night rates and add-ons. A typical medicine and test cost range: needs owner. The tariff validity window must also be reconciled first.
- If my pain flares or there is an emergency, what medical backup is there? Which nearby hospital has MRI and orthopaedic/neurosurgery, and is a physician on campus at night? — _International patients with spine conditions need to know escalation is possible before committing to a rural campus._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ says there are arrangements with nearby hospitals and recommends evacuation insurance. The named hospital, its distance and night physician cover: needs owner.
- Can I manage with limited mobility: a long flight, a 1½–3 hour car transfer, steps to the villa, getting on and off the wooden therapy table, bathroom grab bars, a wheelchair? — _Sitting and transfers are exactly what a disc patient struggles with. Accessibility decides whether they can travel at all._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ mentions ramps and mobility assistance, and /how-to-reach/ asks patients to state mobility needs. Villa-level accessibility and wheelchair availability: needs owner. Note the airport distances disagree between /faqs-downloads/ (75 km) and /how-to-reach/ (~62 km); the owner must reconcile them.
- If my back gets worse and I cannot travel, or I have to leave early, do I lose my 100% advance payment? — _Disc flares are unpredictable. Paying 100% upfront with no refund inside 15 days is the biggest financial fear._ Source: /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ allows medical-emergency exceptions with documentation and management approval, and gives no refund for early departure. This is not stated on this page.
- Who exactly will treat me? What are their qualifications and experience with spine cases, and do they speak English, German, French, Russian or Arabic? — _The page says "our experienced Ayurvedic doctor" but also "Doctor might change as subject to availability" and "multilingual staff" without naming languages._ Source: /online-consultation/ names the physician panel (Dr. Deepu John, Chief Physician, BAMS; Dr. Akhila Oommen, BAMS; Dr. Rahul R, BAMS). Spine-case experience and consultation languages: needs owner.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** Hero, meta and og description: "Heal slip disc naturally … Restore spine health" promises healing and restoration of a structural disc lesion. · The programme name "Spinal Disc & Nerve Restoration Treatment" and the Sanskrit name "Snayu Bala Chikitsa" imply nerve repair. Physician to confirm both names are clinically accurate. · Recurrence and cure claims: "not only heals your spine comprehensively but also rejuvenates it, reducing the chances of recurrence"; "heal the slip disc, relieve pain, and prevent recurrence"; "prevent slip disc recurrence"; "Our approach ensures long-term relief, prevention". · Tissue and disc regeneration: "promote tissue regeneration" (Approach, Process step 05, FAQ 2) and "nourish spinal discs". · "a successful track record of treating 30,000+ slip disc patients" and "proven track record in slip disc treatment": unsourced patient volume and success claim. Scope and evidence are needed from the owner, or remove. · Medication and surgery-avoidance messaging: "reducing the need for pain medications" and "avoid or minimize the use of pain medications and invasive procedures". This needs physician wording plus a red-flag statement (bladder/bowel change, saddle numbness, progressive weakness or foot drop means urgent medical assessment, not this programme). · FAQ 3 safety claim "generally safe as it utilizes natural therapies and herbal medications": the physician must state contraindications and how herbal medicines are checked against existing prescriptions. · "Our ancient Ayurvedic treatments … provide stability and firmness to the affected area" and "offer a tangible solution to spine problems" are efficacy claims. · The therapy protocol differs across sections (Elakizhi/Vasti/Kativasti vs Abhyanga/Pizhichil/Kati Basti/Greeva Basti). The physician must approve one list and say whether "Vasti" (Panchakarma enema) is part of this programme. · The generic day plan includes Udwarthana (herbal powder massage) "to promote detoxification", 30–45 min yoga asanas at 06:30, daily group yoga and a nature walk. The physician must confirm suitability for acute disc or nerve pain, or state that activity is prescribed per patient. · "Ayurveda … views slip disc as a Vata dosha imbalance affecting movement and the nervous system": this traditional framing needs physician approval. · The FAQ 1 definition of a slip disc is imprecise and states symptoms as certain. Physician to approve a corrected definition. · A testimonial-based outcome claim (Mrs. Ashu: "regain the flexibility I had feared I had permanently lost") sits under the heading "Transformative Results". It needs patient consent on file, and the owner and legal must check whether outcome testimonials are permitted in Ayurvedic treatment advertising. · The promise of "Daily Consultation" with a physician (package icons) vs "Daily wellness check-ins": owner and physician to confirm what is actually delivered daily.

### /treatment-for-arthritis-and-spondylitis/ — weak

Osteoarthritis and rheumatoid arthritis: joint pain, stiffness, inflammation and loss of mobility. The goal is a residential programme of 14, 21 or 28 days (minimum 14 nights) under a physician. The slug and two lines mention spondylitis, but nothing on the page covers it. The page content belongs under "Joints, back & spine", beside /treatment-for-cervical-spondylosis/, /treatment-for-chronic-back-pain/, /treatment-for-slip-disc/ and /treatment-for-spondylitis/. It is not a general spondylitis page.

FAQ questions on the page: 4.

**Proofreading (verified):**
- [grammar] “by reducing pain, and inflammation, and improving joint mobility” — FAQ 1 answer has a stray comma after "pain" and a doubled "and". **Fix:** by reducing pain and inflammation and improving joint mobility
- [overclaim-medical] “Yes, Ayurvedic treatment has been proven effective in managing arthritis” — "Proven effective" is an absolute efficacy claim with no citation. It is a medical overclaim on an NABH hospital page. **Fix:** Rewrite with Dr. Deepu John, e.g. "Ayurvedic care may help manage arthritis pain and stiffness; your physician will tell you at consultation what to expect for your condition." Cite a study only if the physician supplies one.
- [locked-word] “Here are the questions guests most often ask before starting the joint rehabilitation and arthritis programme.” — Uses the locked word "guests". **Fix:** Here are the questions patients most often ask before admission to the joint rehabilitation and arthritis programme.
- [overclaim-medical] “Typically, noticeable improvements can be observed within a few weeks, but long-term management may require a more extended treatment plan.” — The timeline is vague and is an outcome claim. "A few weeks" can be longer than the 14-night minimum, so a patient can't tell whether to expect change during the stay. **Fix:** Needs physician: state what a 14-night stay aims for and what continues at home, without promising a timeline.
- [unclear] “It is important to inform your Ayurvedic doctor about any ongoing medications to ensure a holistic and safe approach.” — The only medication answer does not say whether patients on methotrexate, biologics, steroids, NSAIDs or blood thinners should keep taking them. **Fix:** Needs physician: add a clear line such as "Do not stop or change any prescribed medicine without your prescribing doctor; bring your full medication list and our physician will review interactions."
- [overclaim-medical] “If you’re experiencing joint pain, fever, fatigue, and loss of appetite, it could be Arthritis (Osteoarthritis or Rheumatoid Arthritis)” — This invites self-diagnosis. Fever with joint pain can mean infection and needs urgent medical care. "Arthritis" is also capitalised mid-sentence. **Fix:** Needs physician: describe the condition, say a physician confirms the diagnosis at consultation, and add that fever with a hot, swollen joint needs urgent medical attention.
- [overclaim-medical] “They focus on body cleansing, immune system strengthening, and personalized treatments using herbal oils.” — "Strengthening" the immune system is clinically questionable for rheumatoid arthritis, which is autoimmune. "They" has no clear antecedent. **Fix:** Needs physician: replace with the actual treatment aim, e.g. "The programmes combine cleansing therapies, herbal oils and diet, set by your physician."
- [wrong-fact] “With over 118 years of experience treating 30,000+ patients at Kairali – The Ayurvedic Healing Village” — The 118-year heritage belongs to the Kairali Group (founded 1908), not the Village. The harmony brief says never to call the Village 118 years old; the competitor dossier dates KTAHV operations to 1989. The 30,000+ patient count is inherited from a legacy LP and has no locked source. **Fix:** Rewrite as "Kairali’s family practice of Ayurveda dates back to 1908…". Keep the patient count only if the owner confirms the figure and what it covers.
- [locked-word] “We prioritize education, empowering clients to understand and engage in their healing journey.” — Locked word "journey"; "clients" is outside the clinical register. **Fix:** We explain your condition and treatment so patients can take an active part in their recovery.
- [overclaim-medical] “Trust in our expertise for lasting relief and improved vitality.” — Promises a lasting outcome. **Fix:** Delete, or say "Our physicians aim to help you manage symptoms and continue care at home." This needs physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Individuals who prefer natural therapies and medications over conventional treatments” — Rheumatoid arthritis patients could read this as permission to replace their DMARDs or biologics, which is dangerous. **Fix:** Patients who want Ayurvedic care alongside their existing treatment, as agreed with their own doctors
- [overclaim-medical] “Those who want to treat the underlying causes of the issue rather than just the symptoms” — Implies root-cause resolution of a degenerative or autoimmune disease. **Fix:** Needs physician. - [unclear] “Patients seeking a holistic approach to managing arthritis and spondylitis” — Spondylitis is named here and in "Our Approach" but has no content on this page. The site has separate /treatment-for-spondylitis/ and /treatment-for-cervical-spondylosis/ pages, so a patient with ankylosing spondylitis can't tell which page applies. **Fix:** Either remove "spondylitis" and link to /treatment-for-spondylitis/, or have the physician write a short spondylitis section. Decide this when placing the page in the sitemap flow.
