A rehabilitation programme for an existing degenerative neck condition is described as prevention. That contradicts the About and History sections. **Fix:** Rewrite Process for cervical spondylosis: physician assessment including review of imaging, the neck-specific therapies (e.g. Greeva Vasti), posture and exercise guidance, and discharge advice. Physician to author or sign off.
- [grammar] “Why one should opt for this therapy?” — Ungrammatical question. **Fix:** "Why choose this programme?"
- [contradiction] “It focuses on preventing health issues before they arise, promoting a proactive approach to well-being.” — Generic preventive copy. The patient already has the condition, so this contradicts the programme's purpose. **Fix:** Replace with neck-specific reasons approved by the physician.
- [overclaim-medical] “By strengthening the immune system, it reduces the risk of illnesses and boosts the body’s defence mechanisms.” — Unsupported claim about immunity and illness risk, and irrelevant to a neck condition. **Fix:** Delete, or replace with a statement the physician has approved.

They also claim immune strength and, further down, 'longevity', with nothing to back either claim. **Fix:** Rewrite Benefits around aims the physician can stand behind (e.g. 'aims to ease neck pain and stiffness'). Remove the immunity and longevity claims.

Then state clearly whether Panchakarma is included or charged extra.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** Hero promise that the programme 'Includes Greeva Vasti, Abhyangam', which reads as if every patient will get Greeva Vasti. Therapies are prescribed individually, and Greeva Vasti does not appear in the tariff Inclusions. · '...therapies like Greeva vasti (neck therapy) and rejuvenating massages like Abhyangam to alleviate pain and restore mobility' is an outcome claim ('restore mobility'). · 'we aim to reduce inflammation, relieve pain, and improve spinal flexibility' claims a therapeutic mechanism and outcome. · 'reduce stress, which can exacerbate cervical spondylosis' is a causal medical claim. · 'this degenerative spinal condition, which is caused by the wear and tear of the cervical discs' is a statement of cause that needs clinical accuracy review. · 'Treatment is based on balancing the Vata dosha, which is associated with joint degeneration and pain in Ayurveda' is clinical framing that needs physician approval. · The Sanskrit name 'Manyastambha Chikitsa' is presented as the classical match for cervical spondylosis. The physician should confirm the nomenclature; Greeva Graha or Greevastambha are the alternatives. · The programme name uses 'Rehabilitation', which implies a structured rehabilitation or physiotherapy component that the page never describes. · 'Kairali has developed an effective and comprehensive treatment plan that includes Panchakarma detoxification' claims efficacy and says Panchakarma is included, which conflicts with the paid add-on listing. · 'Kairali has helped numerous individuals alleviate the symptoms of cervical spondylosis, promoting long-term relief' is an outcome claim with no data. · 'natural, non-invasive treatments' implies a safety profile. · 'Those interested in curing Cervical' is an implied cure claim and a locked word. · Benefits and 'Why opt' copy: 'By strengthening the immune system, it reduces the risk of illnesses', 'enhances vitality, energy, and immune strength', 'prevents potential health issues', 'longevity'. These are general health claims that are not relevant to this condition. · 'Ongoing support ensures long-term health' is a guaranteed-outcome wording. · The generic daily routine prescribes 30–45 minutes of yoga asanas at 06:30 plus yoga at 17:00, and names Udwarthana as the example therapy. The physician must confirm that this routine is safe and applicable for cervical spondylosis patients, including how yoga is adapted for the neck. · 'Detoxifying Drink ... to kickstart the metabolism' and 'promote detoxification' are physiological claims. · Testimonial under 'Transformative Results' ('I can freely move my neck. My life has been genuinely rejuvenated by Kairali.', attributed only to 'Mr. George ★★★★★ 5.0'). Its provenance and consent need verifying (HARD GATE 6: no unverified reviews presented as genuine), and the outcome heading needs clinical sign-off. · The page has no statement of red flags or contraindications (arm weakness, myelopathy signs, recent trauma) and no statement on continuing current medicines. Any FAQ answer written for these gaps is clinical content that needs Dr. Deepu John's sign-off before staging goes to production. · The 'Who is this for' list covers 'Spondylitis' and 'Shoulder Pain'. The physician should define the clinical scope of this page against /treatment-for-spondylitis/, /treatment-for-chronic-back-pain/ and /treatment-for-slip-disc/.

### /treatment-for-spondylitis/ — weak

Spondylitis: pain and stiffness in the neck, back and lower spine. The programme is Merudaṇḍa Chikitsa, sold as 14, 21 or 28 days with a 14-night minimum. The page mixes up inflammatory spondylitis (for example ankylosing spondylitis) with wear-and-tear spondylosis, and it overlaps with four other spine pages: /treatment-for-cervical-spondylosis/, /treatment-for-arthritis-and-spondylitis/, /treatment-for-chronic-back-pain/ and /treatment-for-slip-disc/.

FAQ questions on the page: 3.

- [grammar] “In order to provide long-term comfort, this also involves yoga poses, stretches, and herbal supplements.” — It is unclear what 'this' refers to. - [overclaim-medical] “These therapies aid in strengthening the spine and related muscles, lowering inflammation, and increasing mobility.” — Oil therapies are said to strengthen the spine and lower inflammation. So the page gives three different treatment lists and spells the same therapies differently (Kati Vasti/Kativasthi, Abhyangam/Abhyanga). **Fix:** Use one physician-confirmed spondylitis therapy list in About, Process, FAQ and routine, with one spelling for each therapy.
- [wrong-fact] “It can result from ageing, poor posture, lifestyle issues, or degeneration, with nerve compression and an increase in Vata dosha being key contributors.” — This describes degenerative spondylosis, not spondylitis. **Must-answer questions missing (9 of 24 gaps):**
- Is this programme for ankylosing spondylitis (inflammatory/autoimmune), for cervical or lumbar spondylosis (wear and tear), or both? Which of your five spine pages is the right one for me? — _A patient with a rheumatologist's AS diagnosis reads a definition of degeneration and a 'spondylosis' package, then finds /treatment-for-cervical-spondylosis/, /treatment-for-arthritis-and-spondylitis/, /treatment-for-chronic-back-pain/ and /treatment-for-slip-disc/ covering similar ground. They can't tell whether they qualify. This is also the fix for the sitemap flow: the spine family needs to be grouped by condition content, not by title._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John to define scope); then route through the Joints, back & spine group in the sitemap to the sibling pages
- I take biologics (TNF or IL-17 inhibitors), methotrexate or sulfasalazine, daily NSAIDs, steroids or blood thinners. Must I stop them, and can your herbal medicines interact with them? — _Stopping these can trigger a flare. It is the most common reason an AS or spondylosis patient over 40 hesitates. The page only says 'Bring: … medications'._ Source: needs physician/owner (Dr. Deepu John). /treatment-for-arthritis-and-spondylitis/ has a generic 'inform your doctor about ongoing medications' line that is not approved for this condition.
- Are Pizhichil, Kati/Greeva Vasti, Elakizhi, steam and daily yoga safe if I have osteoporosis, a fused ('bamboo') spine, earlier spine surgery or implants, neck instability, or numbness or weakness in my arms or legs? Who should not come? — _Fracture and nerve risks are real in advanced spondylitis. The page lists no contraindications and prescribes 30–45 minutes of asanas._ Source: needs physician/owner
- I'm 60+ with high blood pressure, diabetes or a heart condition. Can I tolerate daily oil, heat and steam therapies, and how am I monitored? — _Heat and sweating therapies worry older patients with other conditions. The page doesn't mention age or other conditions at all._ Source: /faqs-downloads/ ('special considerations for senior patients': modified treatments, medical monitoring, mobility assistance; no stated maximum age), plus physician for the spondylitis specifics
- What exactly happens each day of a 14-, 21- or 28-night spondylitis programme: which therapies, how long each takes, who gives them, and when do I see the physician? — _The routine on the page is generic (Udwarthana, one therapy slot, farm and cooking classes) and contradicts 'Daily 2 Therapies'. The patient can't picture the treatment itself._ Source: needs physician/owner (an outline of the spondylitis protocol)
- How soon should I feel a difference, how long does improvement last, and is 14 nights enough at my severity? — _This is the core value question for a long-haul trip. The page promises 'lasting relief' but gives no realistic expectation._ Source: needs physician/owner (no outcome figures may be invented). /faqs-downloads/ only says musculoskeletal programmes are about 14 nights and the duration is set at consultation.
- What will the whole stay really cost: villa nights plus internal medicines, lab tests, Panchakarma if prescribed, and take-home medicines? — _The page asks for 100% advance payment and says 'no hidden fees', but also says the cost of medicines and tests 'can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses'._ Source: needs physician/owner (typical ranges for medicines and tests)
- What will it cost if I come after 31 October 2026? — _The published tariff expires in about five weeks, and the main European and Gulf booking season falls after that date._ Source: needs physician/owner (new tariff). /faqs-downloads/ already cites 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027 validity for the add-ons.
- Who will treat me (names, qualifications, experience with spine conditions), and will they speak German, French, Russian or Arabic, or is there an interpreter? — _'Expert therapists' and 'multilingual staff' don't reassure a patient who wants to know the doctor. Being able to talk to the physician decides whether they book._ Source: /online-consultation/ (Dr. Deepu John, Chief Physician & Head of Department, BAMS; Dr. Akhila Oommen and Dr. Rahul R, BAMS) and /faqs-downloads/ (BAMS training). The languages spoken need physician/owner.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** 'Get lasting relief from Spondylitis with Ayurvedic spine therapy.' promises a durable result. It appears as the hero line, the meta description, the og:description and the JSON-LD WebPage description. · 'to reduce pain, improve mobility, and provide lasting relief from Spondylitis' (About) is another outcome promise, and it credits 'expert therapists' with choosing treatment. · The definition of the condition mixes up spondylitis and spondylosis and names causes ('ageing, poor posture, lifestyle issues, or degeneration, with nerve compression and an increase in Vata dosha being key contributors'). It needs a physician rewrite that states which condition the programme treats. · 'Your Ayurvedic treatment package for spondylosis': the indication named in the package heading doesn't match the page's condition. · Which therapies the spondylitis protocol actually uses: About (Snehan, Kati Vasti, Abhyangam, Shirodhara, Nasyam), FAQ 1 (Abhyangam, Kativasthi, Pizhichil, Elakizhi) and the routine (Abhyanga, Udwarthana) disagree. · FAQ 1 and its FAQPage JSON-LD: 'These therapies aid in strengthening the spine and related muscles, lowering inflammation, and increasing mobility', framed as 'spine therapy for posture correction'. · FAQ 3 and its JSON-LD: 'In order to provide long-term comfort, this also involves yoga poses, stretches, and herbal supplements' (an outcome promise plus unspecified medicines). · 'By strengthening the immune system, it reduces the risk of illnesses and boosts the body’s defence mechanisms' is a general immunity claim and clinically questionable for autoimmune ankylosing spondylitis. The same text is on /treatment-for-cervical-spondylosis/. · The Benefits and 'Why opt' bullets: 'Natural rejuvenation enhances vitality, energy, and immune strength', 'supporting long-term wellness and longevity', 'focuses on preventing health issues before they arise'. These are generic claims with no spine evidence. · History: 'addresses the root causes of potential health issues', 'promoting detoxification through Panchakarma and using specialized herbs to nourish tissues', 'stimulate healing'. · 'continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey' is an unqualified safety and efficacy claim. · Testimonial block: the headings 'Transformative Results' and 'Living Pain-Free: Inspiring Spondylitis Transformations', plus the testimonial's 'conventional therapies weren’t helping' (a comparison with conventional care). Also confirm that the 'Mr. Shashank' review is authentic and that the patient consented to its use. · Daily yoga: 30–45 minutes of asanas at 06:30, yoga again at 17:00 and 'Daily Group Yoga Sessions', offered to spondylitis patients with no statement that it is modified or physician-cleared (a safety risk for a fused spine, flares or osteoporosis). · Detox claims in the routine: Udwarthana 'to promote detoxification', and a 'detoxifying drink to kickstart the metabolism'. · Who the programme is for: 'Relief from Shoulder Pain' and the generic 'Relief from Pain' widen the indication beyond spondylitis. · Post Treatment: 'Ongoing support ensures long-term health' is an outcome promise. · Any new FAQ answer on continuing or stopping biologics or NSAIDs, contraindications, time to results, age limits or other conditions must be written or approved by Dr. Deepu John before staging goes to production. The agent must not draft these facts.

### /treatment-for-psoriasis/ — weak

Psoriasis and chronic skin conditions (Kushtha, shown on the page as "Kushṭham Shamana Chikitsa"). A residential, physician-guided Ayurvedic programme described as 14, 21 or 28 days (villas have a 14-night minimum). The page lists Abhyanga, Takradhara, medicated herbal baths, internal medicines, Panchakarma "as needed", diet and yoga. Note: /treatment-for-skin-diseases/ ("Ayurveda Skin Vitality Revival") also lists eczema and psoriasis, so the two pages overlap.

The same text is in the FAQPage JSON-LD in the view helmet (public/ahv/views/ahv-page-psoriasis-desktop.json and -mobile.json), so it has to be fixed in both places. **Fix:** 'Yes. Patients follow a physician-prescribed diet…', then name the actual excluded food groups (the physician has to confirm the list). Fix the visible FAQ and the JSON-LD together.
- [locked-word] “Each guest receives a tailored diet chart based on their body composition.” — Uses the banned word 'guest'. 'Body composition' is a gym/Western term; in Ayurveda the chart is based on constitution (prakriti) and the condition. **Fix:** 'Each patient receives a diet chart based on their constitution (prakriti) and condition.' This is also in the JSON-LD.
- [locked-word] “Guests are given comprehensive instructions for at-home care, which include nutritional advice, herbal remedies, and lifestyle adjustments.” — Uses the banned word 'Guests'. This is also in the JSON-LD. **Fix:** 'At discharge, patients receive written home-care instructions…'
- [contradiction] “The diet primarily focuses on fresh vegetables, fruits, and readily digestible foods while avoiding dairy, red meat, and processed foods.” — Contradicts the FAQ just above it, which names Takradhara (a buttermilk treatment, i.e. dairy). It also contradicts the rest of the site: the kitchen is vegetarian/vegan and the cancellation policy bans non-vegetarian food, so 'avoiding red meat' suggests meat is otherwise served. **Fix:** Remove 'red meat'. The physician has to state whether dairy is restricted and how that fits with Takradhara (e.g. 'buttermilk is used externally in Takradhara; dietary dairy is advised by your physician').
- [grammar] “The herbal skin treatment for chronic conditions program includes specialized treatments including Abhyanga” — Keyword-stuffed noun string, 'includes… including' repeated, and 'program' instead of 'programme'. 'Internal drugs' (later in the answer) should say 'internal medicines'. **Fix:** 'The psoriasis programme may include Abhyanga (therapeutic oil application), Takradhara, medicated herbal baths and internal medicines, with Panchakarma where the physician prescribes it.'
- [contradiction] “Internal Medicines and test reports needed for your treatment can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses and will cost extra.” — Contradicts FAQ 2 ('includes… internal drugs'), the claim 'All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees!', and /faqs-downloads/, which says packages include 'Herbal medicines and formulations during your stay'. For psoriasis, internal medicine is core treatment, so the patient cannot tell what is included. **Fix:** The owner must decide one rule (included or charged) and state it the same way in the FAQ, the Inclusions list, the T&C and /faqs-downloads/.
- [contradiction] “All-Inclusive Pricing: No hidden fees! Includes accommodation, meals, therapies, and applicable taxes.” — On the same page, extras are charged for medicines, lab tests, Panchakarma, extra food, laundry and internet. 'All-inclusive, no hidden fees' is therefore not true as written. **Fix:** 'Transparent pricing: the nightly rate includes accommodation, meals, prescribed daily therapies and taxes. Medicines, lab tests and Panchakarma are charged as prescribed.' The owner has to confirm this wording.
- [typo] “Does it go on increasing at a commendable rate?” — Wrong word: 'commendable' means praiseworthy. **Fix:** 'Does it keep spreading?' or 'Is it getting worse?'
- [grammar] “Has it shown results even after going through courses of different types of medicines?” — The logic is inverted: it asks whether the condition improved, when it means the opposite. It also implies that other medicines failed. **Fix:** 'Has it persisted despite several courses of treatment?'
- [overclaim-medical] “with its 14/21/28 days treatment venture has come up with a new and exceptional solution to your skin problems” — 'Venture' is odd, it should be '14/21/28-day', and 'new and exceptional solution' is a promotional overclaim for a chronic, relapsing condition. **Fix:** 'offers a 14-, 21- or 28-day physician-led programme for psoriasis and chronic skin conditions.'
- [typo] “The underlined reason can be considered to be the increase of pitta dosha in the body.” — 'Underlined' should be 'underlying'. Clinically, classical texts describe Kushtha as involving all three doshas, and psoriasis is usually correlated with Ekakushtha/Kitibha (vata-kapha). A pitta-only cause needs the physician's wording. **Fix:** 'underlying'. Dr. Deepu John to supply the dosha explanation.
- [overclaim-medical] “The actual reason behind Psoriasis is yet to be known but it is said to be hereditary in nature.” — Medically imprecise. Psoriasis is understood as an immune-mediated condition with genetic predisposition and triggers (stress, infection, some medicines), not simply 'hereditary'. 'Psoriasis' should not be capitalised mid-sentence. **Fix:** Physician-approved one-line description of the condition.
- [unclear] “Ayurvedic Treatment for Psoriasis, rooted in Ayurvedic traditions, offers a holistic approach to optimal health. It emphasizes prevention and rejuvenation to enhance vitality.” — The 'History' section contains no history. It is template copy shared almost word for word with /treatment-for-spondylitis/ and /preventive-and-regenerative-treatment/, with the programme name swapped in, and it says nothing about skin. The Process section (steps 01–05) is equally generic. **Fix:** Replace with psoriasis-specific content (classical Kushtha context, what the skin protocol actually involves) from the physician, or rename/remove the section.
- [locked-word] “Ayurveda’s time-tested wisdom guides the journey to holistic well-being.” — Uses the banned word 'journey'. **Fix:** '…guides treatment toward lasting well-being.'
- [overclaim-medical] “Improves vitality and strengthens the immune system” — Psoriasis is an immune-mediated condition driven by an overactive immune response. 'Strengthens the immune system' (and 'Improved immunity' under Benefits) is clinically inappropriate for this page. **Fix:** Remove, or replace with physician-approved wording (e.g. 'supports balance of the body's response').
- [overclaim-medical] “Prevents health issues by addressing the core cause” — An unqualified prevention/root-cause claim for a chronic, relapsing disease. **Fix:** 'Aims to address contributing factors identified at assessment.' Needs physician sign-off.
- [overclaim-medical] “Balance restoration and prevention of further complications” — Implies the programme prevents complications such as psoriatic arthritis. That is an unsupported outcome claim. **Fix:** Remove, or have the physician word it.
- [grammar] “Empower individuals with a sustainable path to long-term wellness” — Breaks the parallel structure of the list (Offers / Prevents / Improves / Provides). **Fix:** 'Empowers…'
- [locked-word] “with continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey.” — Uses the banned word 'journey'. 'Safe and effective' is also an efficacy claim. **Fix:** '…with continued physician supervision throughout your admission.'
- [grammar] “Doctor might change as subject to availability.” — Grammar. **Fix:** 'Your physician may change, subject to availability.'
- [typo] “Your Ayurvedic Treatment for Psoriasis & Chronic Skin Treatment” — 'Treatment' appears twice. **Fix:** 'Your psoriasis and chronic skin programme'
- [locked-word] “Luxury, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities” — Uses the banned word 'Luxury'. **Fix:** 'Private, vastu-compliant villas with modern amenities'
- [contradiction] “Support: Airport transfers & multilingual staff” — Listed under 'Inclusions', but /faqs-downloads/ says transfers are an extra cost (CJB ₹6,775 one way, etc.). 'Multilingual' names no language, and no page says which languages staff or physicians speak. **Fix:** The owner must confirm whether transfers are included. List the actual languages (owner to supply).
- [locked-word] “Learn about the journey from seed to medicine.” — Uses the banned word 'journey'. **Fix:** 'Learn how a medicinal plant goes from seed to medicine.'
- [locked-word] “socializing with fellow retreat participants” — Hospitality register ('retreat participants'); the required register is 'patients'. **Fix:** '…or time with fellow patients'
- [overclaim-medical] “Healing Skin, Renewing Confidence: Client Testimonials on Psoriasis Treatment at Kairali” — 'Client' breaks the patient register. The section heading 'Transformative Results', over a single unsourced testimonial, is an outcome claim. **Fix:** 'Patient experiences'. Drop 'Transformative Results'.
- [locked-word] “Premium luxury experience” — Uses the banned word 'luxury' (Royal Villa tagline). **Fix:** 'Our most spacious villa' (owner to confirm the descriptor).
- [locked-word] “Inclusions — Your Holistic Wellness Journey Includes” — Uses the banned word 'Journey'. 'Inclusions… Includes' is also redundant. **Fix:** 'What your programme includes'
- [locked-word] “Luxury Stay: Unwind in private, eco-friendly villas designed for tranquility” — Uses the banned word 'Luxury', in holiday register. **Fix:** 'Accommodation: private, eco-friendly villas on the hospital campus'
- [locked-word] “Personalized Care: Daily wellness check-ins with your Ayurvedic doctor to tailor your journey.” — Uses the banned word 'journey'. **Fix:** '…to adjust your treatment.'
- [unclear] “(Note: Preventive Health & Regenerative Program includes one therapy per day.)” — A note about a different programme on the psoriasis page. It confuses the reader about how many therapies they get. **Fix:** Remove it from this page.
- [wrong-fact] “The above rates are valid from April 1, 2025, to October 31, 2026.” — /faqs-downloads/ cites 'current rates (valid 1 Oct 2026 – 30 Sep 2027)'. The two windows overlap in October 2026, and the nightly rates here may be superseded 8 days from today (23 Sep 2026). **Fix:** The owner must confirm which rate card applies from 1 Oct 2026 and update this page's tariff and validity line.
- [contradiction] “Up to 15 days before arrival date – 50% Refund.” — This reads as 0–15 days, which contradicts the next line (under 15 days = no refund). /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ says 50% applies 15–30 days before check-in. **Fix:** '15–30 days before arrival: 50% refund.'
- [grammar] “Up to less than 15 days before arrival date or Change in Date during Stay – No Refund.” — Grammar ('Up to less than'). **Fix:** 'Less than 15 days before arrival, or a date change during your admission: no refund.'
- [locked-word] “NABH Certified Hospital · 118+ Years Heritage · Individualised Programmes · World Luxury Award Winner” — The trust strip uses the banned word 'Luxury' in the site's own claim, not in an award name. 'NABH Certified Hospital' also overstates scope: the awards block says 'NABH-accredited for Panchakarma — certificate AH-2024-0250'. **Fix:** 'NABH-accredited (Panchakarma) · Since 1908 · Individualised programmes' — the owner must confirm the NABH scope wording and remove 'World Luxury Award Winner'.
- [locked-word] “Acknowledged for its outstanding service and guest satisfaction” — The site's own descriptive copy uses the banned word 'guest' (Certificate of Excellence 2018 and 2019 cards). **Fix:** '…for service and patient satisfaction'
- [locked-word] “Celebrated as a leading wellness and spa brand” — The site's own descriptive copy (South Asian Travel Awards 2018) uses the banned word 'spa'. The heading 'South Asian Travel Awards 2018.' also has a stray full stop. **Fix:** 'Recognised as a leading Ayurveda brand…' and remove the stray full stop.
- [typo] “Worlds Luxury Spa Awards Winner 2024” — 'Worlds' is inconsistent with 'World Luxury Spa Awards 2023'; the same error appears in 'Worlds Luxury Hotel Awards 2024'. More broadly, about 25 award cards bring 'Luxury', 'Spa' and 'Resort' ('Wellness Resort of the Year', 'India’s Leading Spa Resort') onto a clinical condition page. These are proper nouns, so the owner has to rule whether they may appear on KTAHV clinical pages. **Fix:** Spell the award names as the awarding bodies do. The owner should rule whether the hospitality awards stay on clinical pages or only on /awards/.
- [typo] “was honored with the CONSIDICI National Award in 2019.” — The heading spells it 'COSIDICI National Award 2019'; the body spells it 'CONSIDICI'. **Fix:** Use the awarding body's official spelling in both places (owner to confirm).
- [locked-word] “onto the back of a guest lying face-down on a wooden treatment table” — The hero image's aria-label uses the banned word 'guest'. The image also shows Udwarthanam, which the page never names as a psoriasis therapy. **Fix:** '…onto the back of a patient…'. The physician must confirm the image shows a therapy actually used for psoriasis.
- [typo] “Psoriasis & Chronic Skin TreatmentKushtham Shamana Chikitsa (Kairali's Treatment for Psoriasis)” — The ogTitle in route-manifest.json has no space between the two titles, and 'Kushtham' lacks the diacritic used in the H1 ('Kushṭham'). **Fix:** 'Psoriasis & Chronic Skin Treatment — Kushṭham Shamana Chikitsa'
- [unclear] “Extra Meals – Breakfast + Lunch + Dinner : INR 5,500.00” — Does not say what the price covers (per person per night, according to /faqs-downloads/). Currency formatting (INR 3,000.00) is inconsistent with the ₹ tariffs. **Fix:** 'Extra meals (breakfast, lunch, dinner): ₹5,500 per person per night'
- [unclear] “For children below 5 years, stay and meals are Complimentary.” — Incomplete compared with /faqs-downloads/ (ages 5–12 charged 50% for food and bedding). Also a stray space in 'Child Policy :'. **Fix:** Add the 5–12 and over-12 rules, or link to the FAQ page.
- [unclear] “Discover More Customizable Programs” — Related programmes point to Spine Care, Preventive Health and Panchakarma, but not to /treatment-for-skin-diseases/ (Skin Vitality Revival), which also lists eczema and psoriasis. The overlapping page, the sensible next click, is missing. 'MORE PROGRAMs' above it is mis-cased. **Fix:** 'More programmes'. Link the skin programme, and state how the two skin programmes differ (owner/physician).

**Must-answer questions missing (9 of 22 gaps):**
- I am on methotrexate, a biologic (adalimumab, secukinumab etc.), cyclosporine, acitretin or strong steroid creams. Do I have to stop them, and can your herbal medicines interact with them? — _This is the first question from most moderate-to-severe psoriasis patients over 40. Stopping systemic drugs or steroids suddenly can trigger a rebound flare. Without an answer they will not book, or their dermatologist will advise against it._ Source: Needs physician. General principle already on /online-consultation/ ('never stop a prescribed medicine without their guidance') and /disclaimer/ ('maintain all ongoing treatments'). A psoriasis-specific answer needs Dr. Deepu John.
- Which types and severities of psoriasis do you accept (plaque, scalp, nail, guttate, pustular, erythrodermic, psoriatic arthritis), and when would you say no? — _Patients with extensive or unstable disease need to know they won't be turned away on arrival. Unsuitable patients should be screened out before they fly._ Source: Needs physician. The page's 'Who is This Program For?' list is generic and has no exclusions.
- What exactly will be done to my skin? Which Panchakarma procedures (Virechana, Vamana, Raktamokshana/leech therapy?), how intense are they, and are they uncomfortable? — _Western patients are put off by the unknown (purging, emesis, bloodletting) more than by the known. The page only names Abhyanga, Takradhara and herbal baths, while the hero image shows a dry powder rub._ Source: Needs physician. The protocol is not stated anywhere on the site.
- How long before I see improvement, can it flare during treatment, and how long do results usually last? Will it come back? — _Psoriasis is chronic and relapsing, so the realistic expectation decides the booking. The page offers only one 'life-changing' testimonial and no timeframe._ Source: Needs physician. No figures may be invented, and any outcome statement needs sign-off.
- What will the total cost be for 14/21/28 nights, including internal medicines, lab tests and any Panchakarma? — _The page says medicines and tests 'can only be ascertained as your treatment progresses and will cost extra'. /faqs-downloads/ says medicines are included, and 'All-Inclusive, No hidden fees' says the opposite. An international patient paying 100% in advance needs a range._ Source: Needs owner: one inclusion rule plus a typical medicine/test range for psoriasis. Nightly tariff is on this page; the rate-card validity conflicts with /faqs-downloads/.
- I am 55–70 with diabetes, high blood pressure, heart disease, fatty liver or obesity (common alongside psoriasis). Is the programme safe for me, and is a doctor on site at night? — _Comorbidities are common with psoriasis at this age. Purgation, heat and oil therapies raise real safety questions for these patients._ Source: Partly on /faqs-downloads/ ('What considerations do you have for senior patients?': medical monitoring, emergency services on call, nearby hospital arrangements). Psoriasis- and comorbidity-specific safety needs physician.
- Who will be my physician, what are their qualifications, and do they speak English, German, French, Russian or Arabic? — _The page says only 'Doctor might change as subject to availability' and 'multilingual staff'. Trust in a foreign doctor is the biggest barrier for a first-time Ayurveda patient._ Source: Physician names and qualifications (BAMS) are on /online-consultation/, and 'Are Ayurvedic doctors real doctors?' is on /faqs-downloads/. The languages spoken need the owner (not stated anywhere).
- If it doesn't help, or I flare badly and need to leave early, do I get anything back? — _With 100% advance payment and 'Change in Date during Stay – No Refund', a patient with an unpredictable condition carries all the risk._ Source: The refund rules are on this page and /cancellation-and-refund-policy/ (which has a force-majeure clause). Any medical-reason exception needs the owner.
- Is this a hospital or a holiday resort? The awards say 'Spa Resort' and 'Luxury'. — _A patient comparing this with a dermatology clinic, or an insurer or visa officer, reads about 25 spa/luxury/resort award cards on a psoriasis page as a wellness holiday, which undermines both clinical trust and a medical visa._ Source: This page: the NABH block (AH-2024-0250, valid to 24 Apr 2027). The owner should rule on moving hospitality awards off clinical pages.

**For Dr. Deepu John:** 'The actual reason behind Psoriasis is yet to be known but it is said to be hereditary in nature.' This describes the cause of the disease and needs physician wording. · 'The underlined reason can be considered to be the increase of pitta dosha in the body.' This gives a dosha cause for psoriasis. Classical texts treat Kushtha as tridoshic, and psoriasis is often correlated with Ekakushtha/Kitibha (vata-kapha). · '…has come up with a new and exceptional solution to your skin problems', together with 'Has it shown results even after going through courses of different types of medicines?', implies superiority over conventional treatment. · Benefits: 'Enhances skin health and decreases symptoms like scaling and itching'. This is a symptom-outcome claim. · 'Improves vitality and strengthens the immune system' and 'Improved immunity and overall vitality' claim an immune-boosting effect in an immune-mediated disease. · 'Balance restoration and prevention of further complications' and 'Prevents health issues by addressing the core cause' are prevention/root-cause claims. · FAQ 1: 14–28 days with 'For best results, the doctors may suggest a lengthier stay'. This needs a physician-set duration and must not imply outcomes. · FAQ 2: the named protocol (Abhyanga, Takradhara, medicated herbal baths, internal medicines, Panchakarma as needed). The physician must confirm the actual psoriasis protocol, and whether Udwarthana (hero image, sample day) is used on psoriatic skin. · FAQ 3: the diet rules ('excludes inflammatory items', 'avoiding dairy, red meat', diet 'based on their body composition'). This contradicts Takradhara (buttermilk) and needs physician correction. · FAQ 4 and 'Continuous post-discharge wellness plan': what home care, herbal remedies and follow-up are actually provided. · 'Panchakarma, a comprehensive detoxification process, helps eliminate toxins from the body' (Process step 04). · '…continued doctor support for a safe and effective journey'. This is a safety and efficacy claim. · The indications list 'Patients with hormonal skin conditions' / 'Individuals with complex skin disorders', with no contraindications stated. The physician should define who is suitable and who is not (e.g. erythrodermic/pustular flares, pregnancy, patients on biologics). · The patient testimonial ('My skin felt calmer and less inflamed…', 'genuinely life-changing') under the heading 'Transformative Results'. This is an outcome claim for disease treatment; the owner must verify the testimonial's source and it needs physician/regulatory review before use. · The Sunday session 'prevent diseases naturally' and the 'Detoxifying Drink… kickstart the metabolism' lines. These are generic health claims repeated on a condition page. · Missing entirely: any statement on existing psoriasis medication (methotrexate, biologics, steroids) — whether to continue it or how to taper. This is the page's highest-risk gap and needs Dr. Deepu John's wording before it can be added.
